*haridāsa kahe,—“mithyā abhimāna kari*  
*tomāra gambhīra hṛdaya bujhite nā pāri*

**Haridāsa Ṭhākura replied, “We are falsely proud of our capabilities. Actually we cannot understand Your deep intentions.**

*kon kon kārya tumi kara kon dvāre*  
*tumi nā jānāile keha jānite nā pāre*

**“Unless You inform us, we cannot understand what Your purpose is or what You want to do through whom.**

*etādṛśa tumi iṅhāre kariyācha aṅgīkāra*  
*eta saubhāgya ihāṅ nā haya kāhāra”*

**“My dear Sir, since You, a great personality, have accepted Sanātana Gosvāmī, he is greatly fortunate; no one can be as fortunate as he.”**

This account illustrates that when a devotee achieves something great in the field of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the devotee must have acted as a cent percent instrument in the hands of the Lord. The Lord then considers the devotee’s body to be His own. *Śrīla Prabhupāda*, You performed extraordinary activities that the Lord wanted done. As Lord Caitanya said, *pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi grāma/ sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma:* “My name will spread to every town and village on the earth.” Lord *Caitanya* fulfilled this most ambitious desire through your body, which He considers His own. Therefore we humbly believe that every word you wrote or said was dictated to you by the Lord. All your words are absolute. Some things we may not understand, but faith in your words is our only means for real success in our life.

*guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete koriyā aikya,*  
*ār nā koriho mane āśā*  
*śrī-guru-caraṇe rati, ei se uttama-gati,*  
*je prasāde pūre sarva āśā*

***“*****Make the teachings emanating from the lotus mouth of our spiritual master one with your heart, and do not desire anything else. Attachment to his lotus feet is the best way to make spiritual advancement. His mercy fulfills all desires for spiritual perfection.”**

And *yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo/ yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ’pi:* Your favor brings Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, and your slight displeasure brings disaster. As you said, *punar mūṣiko bhava:* Without your pleasure, we will again become mice.

Kali-yuga is expanding day by day. The situation is worsening every day. If we don’t get out of this situation, we will have to take another birth in this Kali-yuga. So we pray to you to give us shelter at your lotus feet. *Hā hā prabhu koro doyā, deho more pada-chāyā; jogyatā-vicāre, kichu nāhi pāi, tomāra karuṇā-sāra/ karuṇā nā hoile, kāndiyā kāndiyā, prāṇa nā rākhibo āra.* We have no good qualities; we are simply made of your mercy. If we don’t get your mercy, we will cry and cry and give up our life.

Begging to remain at your lotus feet, we remain  
*Your servants* at ISKCON Ahmedabad, India.

## Allahabad

*Śrīla Prabhupāda*, You Are an Exception  
All the swamis who before went westward  
*Praised the hedonism*, increasing their misconception.  
*It was* only you who showed Kṛṣṇa afterward.  
*Śrīla Prabhupāda*, you are an exception.  
*The sādhus* in India parade on elephants,  
*Smoking gañjā* without restriction.  
*You preached pure devotion*, saying “No intoxicants!”  
*Śrīla Prabhupāda*, you are an exception.  
*The so-called sannyāsīs* are lusty in ripe old age.  
*Following* Rāvaṇa, they simply cause destruction.  
*You trained the youth to get out of the sexual cage*.  
*Śrīla Prabhupāda*, you are an exception.  
*Your godbrothers tried* but utterly failed  
*To revolutioni*ze the Western world’s atheistic notion.  
*With faith in* Kṛṣṇa’s name, you succeeded when you sailed.  
*Śrīla Prabhupāda*, you are an exception.  
*The order of your master* you took to heart  
*And,* like *Hanumān,* crossed the ocean.  
*Now La*ṅkā is burning due to your love dart.  
*Śrīla Prabhupāda*, you are an exception.  
*Puffed up by money, erudition*, and following,  
*Neophytes fall to the illusory attraction*.  
*Always humble* despite of all the knowing,  
*Śrīla Prabhupāda*, you are an exception.  
*“*Eat, drink, and be merry”  
*Is the world*’s motto of sense gratification.  
*You say* “austerity” to stop the transmigratory ferry.  
*Śrīla Prabhupāda*, you are an exception.  
*When wayward disciples are a trouble*,  
*Causing a disturbance to devotion*,  
*You are expert in clearing the rubble*.  
*Śrīla Prabhupāda*, you are an exception.  
*The modes of nature bind one and all*,  
*Making them dance to their direction*,  
*But they can never make you fall*.  
*Śrīla Prabhupāda*, you are an exception.

We beg to always remain  
Your servants at ISKCON Allahabad, India.  
*(*written by Śyāmānanda Dāsa Brahmacārī)

## Amravati

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful and humble prostrated obeisances at your divine lotus feet on this 118th anniversary of your most auspicious appearance day.

Although every moment is meant for your glorification, today is a special day to express our gratitude to you by remembering what we have received from you.

*You appear*ed as a jewel in 1896, O Prabhupāda,  
*Showering benedict*ions on fallen souls at large.  
*You gave the holy name to deliver us from Māyā*’s tyranny,  
*Pouring on us the eter*nal transcendental epiphany.  
Spreading Vedic knowledge through your powerful publications,  
*You have blessed all souls w*ith scriptures so magnificent.  
*You gave us all the pious* Vaiṣṇav association,  
*Which* has uplifted fallen spirits engulfed in depression.  
*O Prabhu*pāda! You have sheltered us in temples, divine destinations,  
*Where one can experience transcende*ntal bliss without hesitation.  
*You have given us the shelter of a* Vaiṣṇav family so extended,  
*Where the whole world can live a life totally cont*ented.  
*You delivered the W*est from the harshest cacophony  
*By manifesting* many *dhāmas* where souls can live in harmony . . .  
*You gave wandering souls what they needed for times unknown*:  
*The real relationship that we have with Lord* Kṛṣṇa alone.

Devotees all over the world have started preparing for the grand golden jubilee celebration of ISKCON in 2016. For more than a year a constant discussion has been going on about the plans and strategies for creating more and more awareness of your unique contribution to the world.

Out of your causeless mercy you tolerated so many difficult situations, but at the same time you showed human society at large that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the practical means for solving all problems. Gradually humanity will discover this most precious jewel that you gave to the world. Human society owes an eternal debt to you, *Śrīla Prabhupāda*, and everybody will acknowledge this very soon.

We followers of yours acknowledge that eternal debt, and understand that we can never repay it. Still, one when a disciple of yours asked how your followers could become free from this eternal debt, you answered, “Do as I am dong.” In other words, take up the preaching mission as our life and soul. So, keeping these words in mind, and recognizing that there is so much to do, we can only paraphrase the famous American poet Robert Frost: “We have many promises to keep, and many miles to go before we sleep.”

And paraphrasing *Śrī* Vallabha Bhaṭṭa’s famous statement from the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Antya* 7.11): *prabhupāda-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana:* “Without being empowered by you, *Śrīla Prabhupāda*, we have no hope of accomplishing anything at all.”

In conclusion, please kindly accept this humble prayer: Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us so that unitedly and sincerely we can all follow your instructions concerning intense chanting of the holy name, in-depth study of *śāstras,* Deity worship, and service to devotees so that we can become your humble instruments in spreading Krsna consciousness near and far.

Your eternal servants at ISKCON Amravati, Maharashtra, India.  
(prose written by Ananta Śeṣa Dāsa, poem by Priyadarśanī Dāsī)

## Austin

My dear most exalted Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the sacred dust of your lotus feet.

Somehow or other, by the causeless mercy of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa and you, my beloved spiritual master, I got the opportunity to surrender my life unto you and your most glorious Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. Serving in your movement has not always been easy, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I have faced many struggles over the years since I joined your movement in 1978. But I know one thing for sure, Śrīla Prabhupāda: whatever difficulties I have faced have all been endured for your pleasure and satisfaction. They have not been for my material sense gratification. Therefore they are all transcendental. In this connection I recall your saying that there is nothing at all intolerable in this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, but even if we think something is intolerable, we must still tolerate it and not go away.

So, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as your fully surrendered servant I am carrying on in my service to you under all circumstances, both the tolerable and the seemingly intolerable, and I am begging your causeless mercy upon this useless fallen soul so that I may never give up your service for all of eternity.

Your lowly servants at ISKCON Austin, Texas, USA.  
(written by Viṣṇupriyā Devī Dāsī)

## Baltimore

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We often hear that your books are the basis. A question arises: What are your books the basis of? As we reflect on this question, we find that they are the basis of everything desirable and valuable in our lives. As we embark on the project of building a new temple and growing our community, we are realizing that the task requires so many elements to come together—knowledge, unity, relationships, clarity, inspiration, organization, funds, progress, and much more. At first glance the project looks daunting, but on deeper reflection we find that you’ve already given us the solution to the difficulties facing us.

To illustrate the point that the best way to serve society is by serving Kṛṣṇa, you often used the analogy that the best way to nourish a tree is by watering its roots. Similarly, we believe that simply by keeping your books in the center—by reading them regularly and applying the knowledge to the best of our ability, and by sharing them with others— everything we need to build our new temple and community will become manifest by your mercy. To this end we started a reading program on the first day of this year. More than twenty-five devotees came together and read from your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and shared realizations. This reading inspired everyone so much that we all took a vow to read a certain number of pages from your books every day. The word spread quickly, and now our combined vows have us reading over 370 pages per day. That amounts to 135,000 pages per year. Devotees who are reading are feeling rooted in their Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and consequently they are feeling more enthusiasm in their chanting and their services. To signify that our temple is built on the foundation of your books, we plan to put the names of those devotees who have taken vows to read every day in the foundation of our new temple.

We pray at your lotus feet that as we progress, as we see the community grow, we may never forget each individual, that as the new temple becomes manifest we may never forget the substance, that as we strive for bigger goals we may never forget our smallness, that as we taste the fruits of your mercy we may never forget the cause, and that rather than striving for big things with small love we may always strive for big love in small things, as you taught by your personal example.

At *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.9.24 Lord Brahma prays, *mā rīriṣīṣṭa nigamasya girāṁ visargaḥ: “*I pray that in the course of my activities I may not be deviated from the vibration of the Vedic hymns.” We similarly pray at your lotus feet that as we embark on many exciting projects we may never be deviated from your books.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are an ocean of mercy. That is a fact, for you liberated thousands of people with your personal association (*vapuḥ*) and are liberating millions more through your books (*vāṇī*). We devotees at New Kulīna-grāma *dhāma*, in Baltimore, are experiencing that fact when we go out to distribute your books and hear the realizations of all sorts of people. Dear *Śrīla Prabhupāda*, on your Vyāsa-pūjā day please bless us that we will always serve your lotus feet with unflinching devotion and faith.

*Your servants* at ISKCON Baltimore, Maryland, USA.

## Bangkok

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Although Your Divine Grace never visited Bangkok, you did have a plan to come here. At the last moment the plan was changed, and instead you wrote a message to the people of Bangkok.

You understood the mentality of the Thai people, especially their love for deities of all descriptions, and therefore you advised us not to be too eager to install Deities here but to make sure that the people first understand the philosophy. Keeping these instructions in our hearts, we are making all efforts to distribute your books and present your teachings throughout Thailand.

Last year we had the good fortune to host a group of Russian devotees who did wonderful *harināma*-*saṅkīrtana* in all the public places of Bangkok. We also held a conference on environmental pollution and presented the Vedic perspective. We are in the process of establishing regular classes for students in two of the leading universities here, and we hope that in the coming year we can distribute larger quantities of *prasādam*.

We have many things still to do before we can claim that we have introduced Kṛṣṇa consciousness to Thailand. We have managed to bring out your *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Kṛṣṇa* book in Thai, but we have not yet printed a single volume of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. We have established a small restaurant for distribution of *prasādam,* but Bangkok is a city of over ten million, and there is a great need for us to expand on our present endeavor.

We have regular morning and evening programs, celebrate all the major festivals, and strictly observe Ekādaśīs. The devotees here are steady in their services and work together enthusiastically. Although they have full-time jobs, they still manage to join our Sunday *saṅkīrtana* and book distribution. We have a grand Ratha-yātrā festival every year, and devotees come from all over the country take part. At the present time Bangkok is not a peaceful city, but our devotees have gained a reputation for bringing a message of peace.

In this way we are trying to establish your mission in a land that is almost totally Buddhist. Please give us some intelligence so that we may serve you as you would like to be served.

We pray that on this auspicious anniversary of your appearance in this world we may be pure in our efforts and remain faithful to your instructions.

Please give us shelter at your lotus feet eternally.  
Your unworthy servants in Bangkok, Thailand.

## Boston

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

The very first place you arrived in America was Boston. The Deities of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīvallabha were directly installed by your lotus hands. You stayed at the temple on North Beacon Street even though the facility wasn’t fit for a pure devotee. When a devotee living outside paid for a suite at an expensive hotel, you refused to go, saying the hotel was in the mode of ignorance and the temple was transcendental. You stayed at the temple with the devotees and set a perfect example. Before you came to Boston the devotees did some last-minute painting, and you pointed out that a paint tray with a brush was left outside and should have been properly cleaned and put away. You were always conscious of your surroundings and of not wasting Kṛṣṇa’s energy. Once you mentioned that if a person slept in a barn he should know how many rafters there were in that barn. In this way you taught us that to be Kṛṣṇa conscious means to be conscious of everything, although you also said, “We can forget everything else, but we should never forget Kṛṣṇa.”

Your whole life was an example for your followers to follow. We can learn from every little thing you ever did or said. Please help us, your Boston devotees, become more Kṛṣṇa conscious and increase Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the first city in America to be graced by the touch of your divine lotus feet as you began your mission of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all around the world.

Hare Kṛṣṇa!

Your servants at ISKCON Boston, Massachusetts, USA.  
(written by Pyāri Mohan Dāsa)

## Botswana

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you on this most auspicious day of your appearance!

On this day we have the opportunity to reflect on all the amazing things you have accomplished. Your hard work goes on through all your amazing disciples who have dedicated their lives to your service. This is possible only because you are a pure and empowered personality.

By the mercy of Your Divine Grace, ISKCON Botswana has gone from strength to strength. Since we opened the new purpose-built temple for Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma, a wide range of new preaching opportunities has opened up.

We welcome the students from at least two schools every month, who visit as part of their religious studies curriculum. They are given a tour of the temple, a concise presentation of our philosophy, *darśana* of our Deities, and some *prasādam* to take with them. These visits are proving very popular, and more and more schools are approaching us.

We have several local outreach programs, including regularly inviting our neighbors for *prasādam* and going out on *harināma* in the neighborhood. These have been very successful. We have a regular Sunday Feast program, like the one you started, and we recently began a Bala Gopal group for our younger members.

We also hold a very successful Food Festival twice a year. While *prasādam* is the main attraction, we have a book stall and gift shop as well, and in this way we appeal to a wide range of people.

All these activities are due only to your mercy and kindness.

*he guro jñāna-da dīna-bandho*  
*svānanda-dātaḥ karuṇaika-sindho*  
*vṛndāvanāsīna hitāvatāra*  
*prasīda rādhā-praṇaya-pracāra*

***“*****O spiritual master, O giver of divine wisdom, O friend of the fallen, you are the giver of your own bliss and are the only ocean of mercy. Although dwelling in Vṛndāvana, you have descended for the welfare of fallen souls like me and are preaching the divine love of Rādhā for Kṛṣṇa. Please be kind upon me.”**

Without your mercy we would be in total ignorance and would waste this most precious human life. “Thank you” is inadequate for all you have given us. I pray that we can continue serving you and this most brilliant ISKCON society, life after life.

Your servants in Botswana.

## Brahmapur

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

By the example of your own life you showed how a devotee should untiringly serve the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. On this auspicious occasion of the 118th anniversary of your appearance, we pray to humbly follow in your footsteps so that our deeds will be pleasing to you and Lord Kṛṣṇa.

This year we plan to start systematic youth preaching so that we can develop strong, dedicated *brahmacārī* and *gṛhastha* *āśramas*. Last year we introduced the Puṣya-abhiṣeka festival in our temple and will try to do better next year.

We are trying to complete the plastering and brick works of our main temple building.

We are trying to put more stress on our morning program so that our brahmacārīs can maintain the highest spiritual standards.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us so that our efforts will be successful and we can better serve you and Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Your insignificant servants at ISKCON Brahmapur, India.

## Brisbane

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Sitting at your lotus feet today, writing this offering, I remember back two years ago when we brought you here to our new address for the first time. It was a triumphant moment—after many struggles and much effort, leading from Bank Rd. to Jennifer St., we carried you in, and it was clear that you were not just being brought here but that you had consented to come.

I look around now and think about what we have accomplished here to offer you: *Śrī-Śrī Gaura-*Nitāi stand on the altar near you, dressed and cared for as though every day were a festival by *pūjārīs* who are well aware that on some days few visitors will even see Them; we are led by a manager who is continuing the legacy of his own compassionate spiritual master and making devotee care a priority; there are devotees you initiated, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who still, in spite of their age, are distributing books, cooking, and showing us how to honor the previous *acaryas’* festival days; there are so many professional people here in our community who work long hours at their day jobs and then continue into the night with service to your ISKCON; there is a handyman who sometimes exchanges his overalls for *dhotī* and *kurtā* and then gives incredible philosophical classes on your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam;* there are young, enthusiastic men and women keeping your traditions of Sunday Feast and *harināma* *saṅkīrtana* alive and thriving; there is a *bhaktin* with a bad leg who drives two hours to come here and make the gardens you look out upon pleasant; there are the youth who are maturing now and by themselves putting on entire huge festivals such as Janmāṣṭamī; and this year, for the first time, there are sincere devotees working to put on Brisbane’s first major Ratha-yātrā, in the heart of the city, with the procession being solely for Lord Jagannātha.

Our Bhakti-vṛkṣa program is nicely training devotees; our Yoga of Success program is a creative way of introducing newcomers; our restaurants distribute tons of *prasādam*—Śrīla Prabhupāda, we could greatly extend this list of devotees who love you and for whom service to you is the highest goal of their lives. Because, Śrīla Prabhupāda, each one of us knows in our heart that you have saved us. Are saving us. Everything we have mentioned above is here because of you: Deities, *prasādam,* books, temples, *kīrtana,* festivals—everything.

With gratitude, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for all you did, all the sacrifices you made that we can never repay, we offer you whatever Kṛṣṇa has given us, to be used in His service. Please consent to using us as instruments as we face the next challenge of building a temple for your pleasure. We hope that one day soon we will be carrying you once more—just next door—to an even larger and grander new residence.

All your humble servants at ISKCON Brisbane, Australia.

## Budapest

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your divine lotus feet!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On the occasion of your most auspicious appearance day, please kindly accept this offering from your sincerely aspiring devotees at the Budapest *yātrā*.

Fully aware that the glories of the pure devotees of the Lord are beyond the limits of the words uttered by a materially contaminated person, I still hope that both you and the Lord may find some pleasure in this imperfect attempt.

Unless we are cautious, we who live in a blissful community of devotees can too easily forget what it means to be a devotee. We may forget what a rare and fortunate situation we are in.

The *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (6.14.5*)* says:

*muktānām api siddhānāṁ nārāyaṇa-parāyaṇaḥ*  
*su-durlabhaḥ praśāntātmā koṭiṣv api mahā-mune*

**“O great sage, among many millions who are liberated and perfect in knowledge of liberation, one may be a devotee of Lord Nārāyaṇa, or Kṛṣṇa. Such devotees, who are fully peaceful, are extremely rare.”**

And not only fully peaceful, but perfectly fearless:

*nārāyaṇa-parāḥ sarve na kutaścana bibhyati*  
*svargāpavarga-narakeṣv api tulyārtha-darśinaḥ*

**“A pure devotee of Nārāyaṇa is never afraid of going anywhere and everywhere. For him heaven and hell are one and the same.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **6.17.28)**

Blinded by pride, the average Westerner considers the Orient backward, underdeveloped, and underprivileged while admiring his Western world for its prosperity and progress. Discoveries of modern science, combined with the educational system, have convinced people that most problems can be solved by science and that even death will soon be defeated. Science sells the mirage of hope: “Everything will be all right. Just enjoy life and don’t care about anything.” This strong faith of the materialists is the reason why they do not see that what they consider prosperity is, in reality, poverty, that their so-called progress is leading them to hell. The depth of their faith—their illusion—is shown by how desperately and bravely they defend their baseless and absurd tenets.

*yā niśā sarva-bhūtānāṁ tasyāṁ jāgarti saṁyamī*  
*yasyāṁ jāgrati bhūtāni sā niśā paśyato muneḥ*

**“What is night for all beings is the time of awakening for the self-controlled; and the time of awakening for all beings is night for the introspective sage.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **2.69)**

You told us that the main fault of this world is that although no one is perfect everyone thinks he is, and not only perfect but the most perfect—the supreme, God. Since everyone more or less shares this view, these many “godlets” have created a so-called civilized society based on pride, envy, competition, and corruption. This “advanced” Western realm is the perfect manifestation of the dark side of the material world, a world your beloved spiritual master compared to a toilet. Gentlemen leave such places as soon as possible because they feel uncomfortable there. What do the pure devotees of the Lord have to do with such a dirty and dark place, where no one has even heard of Him?

Our *śāstras* describe the qualities of the pure devotees in many places. Because such devotees are individuals, their individual qualities make them exceptional and special. Their loving relationship with Kṛṣṇa is also individual, and He reciprocates with each of them uniquely. Among the colorful jewels of these wonderful qualities, one is common among them. It is the effulgent gem of devotion. This jewel, unconditional love of God, is solid, deep and irrevocable, and is the possession of every pure devotee. Loving devotion can inspire a devotee to perform deeds that amaze even the mundane moralist.

*sarvathā dhvaṁsa-rahitaṁ saty api dhvaṁsa-kāraṇe*  
*yad bhāva-bandhanaṁ yūnoḥ sa premā parikīrtitaḥ*  
*(Ujjvala-nīlamaṇi 14.63)*

A devotee’s love for Kṛṣṇa is pure when he remains faithful even though he has every reason to cut off his relationship with the Lord. This pure love inspires the devotee to surrender with his whole being. This is incomprehensible to the materialist because understanding such devotion is beyond the limits of mundane logic. What does such a person, perfect in every respect, have to do in this hellish world? Why did he come here?

Of course, we cannot refer to you as being here in the sense of an ordinary person being a part of this world. Scripture tells us that saintly persons, although they seem to be here with us, are always with God in His realm. Therefore the material world has no power over them. The scriptures compare the feet of such saintly persons to lotus flowers. This indicates that just as the petals of a lotus flower are never touched by the water the lotus grows in, the lotus feet of the pure devotee never touch this land of illusion, impermanence, and darkness.

That said, we should not think that the pure devotee undergoes no difficulties, hardships, impediments, or suffering. That belief would cast doubt over the concept of love. Those who walk the path of God have to face many difficulties, by which they get ample opportunity to express and deepen their love and devotion for Him. The difference between them and the materialists is that, unlike the latter, under no circumstances do they ever forget Kṛṣṇa, and therefore they are under His protection at all times.

Therefore the crest jewels of the pure devotees, the *paramahaṁsas,* appear in this material world for the pleasure of the Lord and out of mercy toward the conditioned souls, and thus they work to ease the burden of the world even in the face of severe hardship.

You did not come to this world as we did. You never fell down, never committed any sins, and never turned away from Kṛṣṇa. As you told your most confidential disciples, you came on the personal request by Kṛṣṇa, and although you knew that this is hell, you agreed. You undertook this mission for Kṛṣṇa, and for us.

You showed what love beyond words is. You gave yourself to Kṛṣṇa as He desired while living the life of an ordinary person. Then you met your eternal well-wisher, your spiritual master, and received your mission from him. He initiated you and you quickly manifested your perfection. After finishing with householder life, you took up residence in your eternal abode, Vṛndāvana, and prepared for your great mission in the temple of *Śrī* *Śrī* Rādhā-Dāmodara.

What a pastime! What a wonderful drama! Kṛṣṇa is the greatest director, and His cast is always the best.

Then, encouraged at every moment by this wonderful director, you arrived in the land of darkness. Although nothing was easy, by your devotion, humility, and love you achieved success in your mission. You made a miracle.

Free will is the only possession of the spirit soul. Depending on how he uses it, Kṛṣṇa provides various facilities for him. Although everything comes from Him—everything is His energy and nothing is independent of Him—still He does not influence the living beings in exercising their free will. In the spiritual world He provides every soul with the bliss of eternal relationships, full of knowledge, but also the possibility of forgetfulness leading one to take up the role of independent enjoyer in the material world. The *jīva* receives all this from Kṛṣṇa, whether or not he acknowledges it. The Lord does not interfere with the *jīva*’s free will, because true love can be based only on free will. Love cannot be coerced but must be given freely. Kṛṣṇa is waiting patiently for the *jīva* to express such love toward Him again. This is His supreme desire, for He fervently loves his devotees even if they turn away from Him.

Great *yogīs* and sages can use their mystic powers to enchant the ignorant, but such miracles do not benefit the living beings. The real miracle is to change the heart of the living beings, to induce them to use their free will to direct their love toward Kṛṣṇa again. You turned Kali’s rogues into pure devotees of the Lord, who are effulgent with good qualities.

No one but a pure, empowered devotee of the Lord could do this. You fulfilled the prediction that the holy name of Kṛṣṇa would spread all over the world. How dear you must be to the Lord! What an intimate relationship you must have with Him!

No one can find the limits of your glorious qualities, no matter how hard one might search throughout the eons. My purpose here has been to highlight the crest jewel of your qualities: your infinite loving devotion to Kṛṣṇa, along with its consequent fathomless compassion for the suffering *jīvas*.

Such a pure devotee, a *siddha*, cannot be deterred but is determined to satisfy the Lord in any circumstance. This is a rare quality even among those who have achieved perfection. Words fail to express how very fortunate we are just to be in contact with you. Dear *Śrīla Prabhupāda*, you are the most wonderful devotee.

If you consider us worthy and are at all satisfied with our service, please help us increase our devotion and love for you and the Lord of your life!

Please shower your mercy on us so that someday we will also become pure devotees and perfect servants of your perfect mission!

Your insignificant servants at ISKCON Budapest, Hungary.  
(written by Candragupta Dāsa)

## Canberra

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Vyāsa-pūjā is the celebration of the appearance of *Śrī* Guru, the spiritual master. His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami *Prabhupāda* is not an ordinary guru, for he is the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON. As the founder-*ācārya* of the ISKCON institution, Śrīla Prabhupāda gives the directions on how to understand and apply the teachings of *Śrīmad Bhagavad-gītā*, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* and *The Nectar of Devotion*. The *ācārya* teaches by example how to practice *bhakti-yoga,* known to us as Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Being a pure devotee of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa and empowered by Lord Caitanya, Śrīla Prabhupāda gave Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the world at a time when India was trying to forget about Kṛṣṇa. Indeed, the first Prime Minister of India, Jawaharlal Nehru, thought that India’s progress was being impeded by the people’s belief in God and their general spiritual inclination. He even stated that it would be better to turn the temples into factories to achieve prosperity.

*yad yad ācarati śreṣṭhas tat tad evetaro janaḥ*  
*sa yat pramāṇaṁ kurute lokas tad anuvartate*

**“Whatever action a great man performs, common men follow. And whatever standards he sets by exemplary acts, all the world pursues.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **3.21)**

So the leaders were trying to stop the progress of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in India, and the common people were influenced by this effort.

In 1965 the United States of America was the world leader in terms of overall prosperity, standard of living, and material achievement. Śrīla Prabhupāda thought, “If I can make the Americans Kṛṣṇa conscious, then the whole world, including India, will surely follow.” Thus he traveled to America in 1965 and began preaching the pure message of Lord Caitanya, and ISKCON was founded a year later with the express purpose of spreading the teachings of Lord Caitanya all over the world. In 1970 Śrīla Prabhupāda, accompanied by a small band of Western disciples, returned to India and began preaching there, with great success.

We cannot overestimate the potency of the teachings Śrīla Prabhupāda presented:

*kali-kālera dharma—kṛṣṇa-nāma-saṅkīrtana*  
*kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana*

**“The fundamental religious system in the Age of Kali is the chanting of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Unless empowered by Kṛṣṇa, one cannot propagate the** ***saṅkīrtana*** **movement.”**

Being an empowered representative of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, Śrīla Prabhupāda imparted the unadulterated teachings of the Lord. He made it clear that his only qualification was that he did not change the pure message of *Śrīmad Bhagavad-gītā,* and that everything was the mercy of Śrīla *Bhaktisiddhānta* Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. In this way Śrīla Prabhupāda acted as the transparent medium through which the pure teachings of Lord Caitanya were transmitted through the *paramparā,* or disciplic succession. One can act as a transparent medium only if one has no desire separate from Lord Kṛṣṇa’s—or, in other words, if one has no material desires and is completely surrendered to the lotus feet of the Lord. This was Śrīla Prabhupāda. He started ISKCON for no reason other than to serve Lord Kṛṣṇa, and personally he was never compromised by the material facilities that came with the success of being the head of an international society. His Divine Grace’s sole purpose was always to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness, never to enjoy the fruits of his work. Śrīla Prabhupāda never used any of the money that came with success for sense gratification, although he worked tirelessly. In fact, His Divine Grace worked harder than any of his disciples, who were all in the prime years of their life. He was old enough to be their grandfather, but he worked harder than any of them.

In Śrīla Prabhupāda we saw all the qualities of a spotless, pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa, unaffected by the modes of material nature, ecstatically executing devotional service, full of enthusiasm, not wasting a minute that could be used for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. The activities and qualities of the pure devotee described in the verses of *Śrī Śrī Gurv-aṣṭaka* were present in the person of Śrīla Prabhupāda. Some of us were fortunate to see the *mahā-bhāgavata* in person. To actually witness the pure devotee behaving in exactly the way described in the revealed scriptures is a rare blessing. This was certainly very special mercy given by Lord Caitanya and Lord Kṛṣṇa to the disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda. For the devotees who have joined ISKCON after the disappearance of Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is absolutely necessary to stick very closely to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s teachings and example, without making any changes. In this way they can have the same result as those who were present during his preaching *līlā.*

Once, after Śrīla Prabhupāda had visited a temple, a disciple from that temple wrote him a letter expressing how devastated he and the other devotees there were after *Śrīla Prabhupāda* had left their temple and gone to his next destination. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s answer was that there are two forms of the spiritual master, *vāṇī* and *vapuḥ*. *Vāṇī* consists of the spiritual master’s teachings, and *vapuḥ* is his physical presence. Of the two forms of Śrī Guru, *vāṇī* is more important, because while the spiritual master’s physical form will go away, his teachings will stay forever, and the disciple can always feel his presence in his teachings if he remains faithful and dedicates himself to living and spreading those teachings of the *paramparā*.

The extent of *Śrīla Prabhupāda*’s achievement in giving the teachings of Lord Caitanya to the world cannot be overestimated, and as time goes by we will see more and more just how great was the contribution of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda to the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants at ISKCON Canberra, Australia.  
(written by Svayambhūr Dāsa)

## Cape Town

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet on this most auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration.

In the eighth verse of his *Śacī-sutāṣṭakam,* Śrīla Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya writes:

*aruṇaṁ nayanaṁ caraṇaṁ vasanaṁ*  
*vadane skhalitaṁ svaka-nāma-dharam*  
*kurute su-rasaṁ jagataḥ jīvanaṁ*  
*praṇamāmi śacī-suta-gaura-varam*

**“His eyes, the soles of His feet, and His clothing are reddish like the color that heralds the rising sun. As He utters His own names, His voice falters. Throughout the universe He awakens the sweet flavor of life. I bow down to Gaura, the beautiful son of Mother Śacī.”**

While meditating on Your Divine Grace and remembering this beautiful verse glorifying Lord Caitanya, it occurred to me that just as Lord Caitanya awakened the sweet flavor of life by appearing in this world, you have also awakened the same sweet flavor by taking the message of Lord Caitanya to the whole world. The sweet flavor of life that Śrīla Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya refers to is pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Śrīla Advaita Ācārya Prabhu felt great distress upon seeing the absence of devotion to Lord Kṛṣṇa in the world, and especially in Navadvīpa, which was a great center of learning in India at the time. Śrīla Vṛndāvana Dāsa Ṭhākura tells us:

Many people came from various provinces to study in Navadvīpa, because by studying there one achieved a taste for education. Therefore no one could count the innumerable students and millions of teachers gathered there. By the merciful glance of Ramā, the goddess of fortune, everyone lived happily there, but they wasted their time in mundane activities. Even the so-called scholars—the Bhaṭṭācāryas, Cakravartīs, and Miśras—did not know the real purport of the scriptures. \[*Caitanya-bhāgavata,* *Ādi-kāṇḍa* 2.60–63, 67)

Seeing the absence of devotion to Lord Kṛṣṇa, Śrīla Advaita Ācārya Prabhu felt great distress and knew that only the Lord Himself could correct the situation by personally appearing. Therefore constantly offered *tulasī* buds in water from the Ganges and loudly called on the Lord, and in this way Advaita Ācārya caused the Lord to appear.

As Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja *Gosvāmī* tells us, Lord Caitanya desired to deliver the whole universe, yet although He is undoubtedly Kṛṣṇa Himself, He spread the chanting of the holy name only throughout India.

In His incarnation as Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa descended to deliver all the living beings in the three worlds, from Brahmaloka down to Pātālaloka. He caused their deliverance in three ways. The Lord delivered the fallen souls in some places by meeting them directly, in other places by empowering a pure devotee, and in still other places by appearing before someone Himself. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu delivered almost all the fallen souls by directly meeting them. He delivered others by entering the bodies of great devotees, such as Nakula Brahmacārī. And He delivered still others by appearing before them, as in the case of Nṛsiṁhānanda Brahmacārī. “I shall deliver the fallen souls.” This statement characterizes the Supreme Personality of Godhead. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* *Antya*-*līlā* 2.3–6\]

From these verses, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can understand that you are without doubt Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s eternal associate and that He has therefore caused Your Divine Grace to appear in this material world. He has also appeared in your body to deliver all the living entities in this world. Lord Caitanya Himself said:

*pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi grāma*  
*sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma*

**“In every town and village of the world, the chanting of My name will be heard.” (*****Caitanya-bhāgavata,*** ***Antya-kāṇḍa*** **4.126)**

Just as Śrīla Advaita Ācārya felt great distress to see the lack of Kṛṣṇa’s devotional service in this world, you also expressed great compassion upon arriving in Boston in 1965. Onboard the *Jaladuta* you wrote a Bengali poem, *Mārkine Bhāgavata*-*dharma,* “Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the West.” An excerpt:

My dear Lord Kṛṣṇa, You are so kind upon this useless soul, but I do not know why You have brought me here. Now You can do whatever you like with me. But I can guess that you have got some business here; otherwise why should You call me here \[to a place\] which is demoniac? Most of the population here is covered by ignorance and passion modes of nature, and I do not know how they will be able to understand the transcendental messages of Vāsudeva. But I know Your causeless mercy can make everything possible because You are the most expert mystic. Therefore, I am simply praying for Your mercy so that I can be able to convince them about Your message. All living entities have come under the control of the illusory energy by Your will, and therefore, if You like, by Your will they can also be released from the clutches of illusion. If You so desire, I wish that You may deliver them all, and only by Your such desire all of them will be able to understand Your message. \[*Śrīla Prabhupāda*’s translation, from a letter to Satsvarūpa, 28 April 1970\]

You have fulfilled the prophecy of Lord Caitanya by awakening the sweet flavor of life wherever you went in the world. But more than this, you have made many pure devotees who are also awakening that same sweet flavor of life wherever they go. Also, we can understand that your glorious ISKCON movement will go on generating pure devotees, who will awaken that sweet flavor of life for many thousands of years to come. Therefore we find it impossible to fully understand your greatness and how dear you must be to Lord Caitanya, so much so that He reserved this incredible achievement for you.

Meditating in this way, we, your humble followers in Cape Town, sincerely pray at your lotus feet that we may be able to do some small service to assist you in this great endeavor.

Your humble servants at ISKCON Cape Town, South Africa.

## Chandigarh

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Respected Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful obeisances unto your divine lotus feet.

Once again we have the opportunity to glorify Your Divine Grace for giving meaning to our lives, nay, for giving us life itself. This time we would like to bathe in one of the unlimitedly glorious and profound teachings of Your Divine Grace one that is very natural and simple and yet most important. In fact, this teaching pleased the heart of Śrīla *Bhaktisiddhānta* Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, who, being very much moved by your writing, gave this instruction to the editor of the *Harmonist* magazine: “Whatever he writes, publish it.” We are referring to the fundamental teaching that God is a person, that the living entities are also individual persons, and that the process of *bhakti* is therefore a personal process. You penned this philosophical truth in a poetic stanza glorifying Śrīla *Bhaktisiddhānta* Sarasvatī Ṭhākura on his Vyāsa-pūjā day:

Absolute is sentient  
Thou hast proved.  
Impersonal calamity  
Thou hast removed.

There have been so many inspirations for devotees to write Vyāsa-pūjā offerings to you, *Śrīla Prabhupāda*, but the main inspiration has been the joy of discovering one’s own personality by the means of understanding the personality of Kṛṣṇa through the bona fide spiritual master. As we learn from *Śrīmad*-*Bhāgavatam* 1.2.6: “The supreme occupation \[*dharma*\] for all humanity is that by which men can attain to loving devotional service unto the transcendent Lord. Such devotional service must be unmotivated and uninterrupted to completely satisfy the self.” Or in other words, one who renders unmotivated and uninterrupted devotional service to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa is completely satisfied. How can a devotee know Kṛṣṇa is satisfied with his service? He will feel deep satisfaction in his own heart.

Now, to an impersonalist, or one who is not acquainted with his own heart or personality, the check of one’s own satisfaction would be quite bewildering. Since he is unable to relate to Kṛṣṇa’s heart, how could he ever possibly relate to his own heart?

People in general, being ignorant of their identity, are not satisfied. Then how would a dissatisfied heart satisfy or please others? In your preface to *The Nectar of Devotion,* you compassionately explain how to satisfy not only ourselves but everyone else as well:

*The basic principle of the living condition is that we have a general propensity to love someone.* No one can live without loving someone else. This propensity is present in every living being. Even an animal like a tiger has this loving propensity at least in a dormant stage, and it is certainly present in the human beings. *The missing point, however, is where to repose our love so that everyone can become happy.* At the present moment the human society teaches one to love his country or family or his personal self, but there is no information where to repose the loving propensity so that everyone can become happy. That missing point is Kṛṣṇa, and *The Nectar of Devotion* teaches us how to stimulate our original love for Kṛṣṇa and how to be situated in that position where we can enjoy our blissful life. *The Nectar of Devotion* will teach us how to turn the one switch that will immediately brighten everything, everywhere. *One who does not know this method is missing the point of life*. \[Italics added\]

As explained above, people do not know that they should repose their love in Kṛṣṇa, and hence they are always dissatisfied at heart. And society, being a collection of such dissatisfied hearts, is day by day becoming filled with people who are more and more frustrated and inhumane. The hippies in the 1960s grossly exhibited this frustration, but the situation today is worse. People are not hippies externally by appearance, but internally they are so—quarrelsome, lazy, misguided, unlucky, and above all, always disturbed (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.1.10).

As the true and eternal well-wisher of everyone, you made available the teachings of the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad*-*Bhāgavatam* to provide light for the people of this dark age. You presented the scriptural teachings very practically:

Everything that exists is a product of matter and spirit. Spirit is the basic field of creation, and matter is created by spirit. Spirit is not created at a certain stage of material development. Rather, this material world is manifested only on the basis of spiritual energy. This material body is developed because spirit is present within matter; a child grows gradually to boyhood and then to manhood because that superior energy, spirit soul, is present. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* 7.6, purport\]

So it is spirit that moves matter. Any machine or process is actuated by the spirit or personality behind it; even the most automatic machine needs the intervention of spirit by way of a command to begin operating.

Anyone who understands such a simple presentation will not be deluded by the word jugglery and complex expressions of the scientists concerning the evolution of life or the mysteries of the origin of species, etc. As you would often say, “What is the difficulty?” Since rendering devotional service to Kṛṣṇa is the natural, constitutional position of the living entity, it is not an artificial imposition on the mind or some sort of intellectual adjustment. What is the proof? One feels satisfied at heart when applying his heart to the process of *bhakti*.

So how can one understand his identity and the identity of the Supreme Personality of Godhead? You explain how in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.1.10:

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is recognized Vedic wisdom, and the system of receiving Vedic knowledge is called *avaroha-panthā,* or the process of receiving transcendental knowledge through bona fide disciplic succession. For advancement of material knowledge there is a need for personal ability and researching aptitude, but in the case of spiritual knowledge, all progress depends more or less on the mercy of the spiritual master. The spiritual master must be satisfied with the disciple; only then is knowledge automatically manifest before the student of spiritual science.

You give a similar instruction in your purport to *Bhagavad-gītā* 4.34:

One has to approach a bona fide spiritual master to receive the knowledge. Such a spiritual master should be accepted in full surrender, and one should serve the spiritual master like a menial servant, without false prestige. Satisfaction of the self-realized spiritual master is the secret of advancement in spiritual life.

In summary, to get true knowledge of the scriptures one should approach a bona fide spiritual master. The purports above do not just describe the process of *bhakti* but express the heart of one who has lived such a life—you, *Śrīla Prabhupāda*. The poetic stanza you wrote—“Absolute is sentient, Thou has proved”was not just a mere poem but a personal glorification of Śrīla *Bhaktisiddhānta* Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, written out of genuine worship and joy at having accepted a genuine spiritual master.

The whole process of *bhakti* therefore depends on approaching the personality of Kṛṣṇa through the personality of the bona fide spiritual master. One has to follow the process, and the process is to follow a pure devotee of the Lord.

Once one of your African disciples left the movement, thinking he would attain love of Godhead by chanting a lakh of holy names every day. You commented that the idea that Kṛṣṇa will automatically give *prema* if one does such-and-such is Karma-mīmāṁsā philosophy. Kṛṣṇa is not obliged to give *prema* to anyone, no matter how many rounds he chants.

We come back to the theme we began with: we are individuals, and we need an individual approach to the Personality of Godhead. The ninefold process of devotional service has to be followed, but under the direction of an expert bona fide spiritual master; otherwise one will simply be victimized by Karma-mīmāṁsā philosophy, i.e., thinking that when one performs his duties nicely Kṛṣṇa has to award good results. No. Rather, while following all the details of the process rigidly, one has to consciously seek the pleasure of Kṛṣṇa. And the proof of achieving Kṛṣṇa’s pleasure is that one’s own heart is engladdened.

Seeing *bhakti* in its pure and natural form removes all sorts of confusions, contradictions, and deviations on the path of *bhakti*. All our questions or doubts—concerning creation or evolution, the hen or the egg, mercy or endeavor, energy or the energetic, etc.—are easily resolved by this natural approach, i.e., the personal approach to spirituality: the person brings the process, or the *bhakta* delivers Bhagavān, as is told by Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura: *kṛṣṇa se tomāra, kṛṣṇa dite pāra, tomāra śakati ache.* “Kṛṣṇa is yours; you have the power to give Him to me.” Or as you put it in your purport to *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* *Ādi* 11.59: “A pure Vaiṣṇava, as the proprietor of Kṛṣṇa and love of Kṛṣṇa, can deliver both to anyone and everyone he likes.”

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for offering us Kṛṣṇa and love of Kṛṣṇa through the *guru*-*paramparā* system. You received the personality of Kṛṣṇa from your eternal spiritual master and awarded us the facility to receive Kṛṣṇa in the same *paramparā*.

Following your instructions to please Kṛṣṇa, we are trying to do our bit at ISKCON Chandigarh. The center is stable and moving forward steadily. We distributed 32,000 *Bhagavad-gītās* during the 2013 marathon, held four Ratha-yātrās last year, and added one Bhakta Prahlad School (BPS) to the already existing four BPSs. About eighty devotees are ready for first initiation. We held one T.T.C.I. seminar conducted by M.I. Regular spiritual courses are being held every week for young boys, young girls, and *gṛhastha* devotees. Six young devotees (including four engineers) joined the *āśrama* with the intent of dedicating their lives to your mission as *brahmacārīs*.

All this has been possible by your blessings, the guidance and encouragement of senior Vaiṣṇavas, and the mercy of our presiding Deities, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava. Kindly continue showering your blessings upon us so that we may succeed in our efforts to please Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

The servants of your servants at ISKCON Chandigarh, India.

## Chennai

O worshipable Śrīla Prabhupāda! O dear Grand-Gurudeva!

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you!

Only by your causeless mercy have I been given the opportunity to write this glorification of you in honor of this most blessed occasion, your appearance day. You are the perfect transparent medium to receive and transmit Kṛṣṇa’s mercy because you always act as a selfless instrument in His hands to accomplish His mission. Such an instrument never has any self-motivation: his the only motivation is to please Kṛṣṇa.

I appreciate your description of Kṛṣṇa as perfect and complete. He is completely perfect and perfectly complete. After reading your books, I have concluded that you are also perfect and complete—that is, you are the perfect and complete representative of Kṛṣṇa. Therefore I have the strong hope that if I can get your mercy by pleasing you I will certainly get Kṛṣṇa’s mercy one day.

Your books are the source of all knowledge. Anyone can easily understand your purports and imbibe the principles of devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa. One who follows those principles will easily purify himself, such a pure soul can easily preach the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and such a preacher can easily understand the essence of the mission of Lord Kṛṣṇa, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, you, and all the previous ācāryas!

The fruit of potent preaching is sincere disciples. I am fortunate to have one of those sincere disciples of yours, His Holiness Rādhānātha Swami, as my *gurudeva*. Unfortunately, I am still struggling to become fully submissive to my Guru Mahārāja. Still, I aspire to become a better instrument in your hands for the accomplishment your mission. Hence today, your divine appearance day, I humbly fall at your lotus feet and beg you to bless me so that I may become more submissive and help all Vaiṣṇavas in this ISKCON movement of yours. On my own it is not possible, but by your mercy and your representatives’ mercy it is certainly possible.

Again I beg you to bless me so I will become your perfect servant.

Thank you very much.

All glories to you! All glories to all Vaiṣṇavas!

Your servants at ISKCON Chennai, India.  
(written by Parameśvara Dāsa)

## Coventry

Our dearest and most caring ever well-wisher Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you on your glorious Vyāsa-pūjā day!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* Śrīla Vyāsadeva describes the condition of the people of Kali-yuga: They are short-lived, quarrelsome, lazy, misguided, unfortunate, and always disturbed. With these disadvantages, people are very disinclined to inquire about the real purpose of life, much less take up some practice to pursue it. With our own eyes we have seen that with the progress of Kali-yuga this disinclination is increasing day by day. Even those who initially make good progress often stop making progress and stay where they are or even go backwards. In such an atmosphere, how is it possible to seriously turn toward Kṛṣṇa?

The *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* recounts how Lord *Caitanya* sent His two generals *Rūpa* *Gosvāmī* and Sanātana *Gosvāmī* to Vṛndāvana, how He sent Lord Nityānanda to Bengal, and how Lord *Caitanya* Himself went to South India to broadcast the holy names of Lord Kṛṣṇa in every town and village and in this way distribute *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* and *kṛṣṇa-prema*.

But what about the rest of the world? For this we had to wait another five hundred years—we had to wait for you.

In the *Caitanya-maṅgala* Śrī Locana Dāsa Ṭhākura writes:

\[Lord Caitanya said:\] “Taking the sharp sword of *nāma*-*saṅkīrtana*, I will root out and destroy the demoniac mentality in the hearts of all the conditioned souls. If some sinful people escape and, giving up dharma, go to far-off countries, my *senāpati-bhakta* will go there \[to deliver them\].”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are that *senāpati-bhakta,* that devotee general, who took mercy upon the whole world and came to point us in the right direction. Knowing that almost all people were deeply engrossed in temporary activities for their bodies, and that there was so much misguidance, you traveled around the world fourteen times to give us true guidance and show us what true spirituality is. For this most compassionate act of yours, we and the whole world owe you a great debt that can never be repaid.

We in Coventry are certainly relishing your blessings, and we thank you very much for twenty years ago sending us our beloved Lordships Śrī Śrī Śacīnandana and Nitāicānda, whom we have been worshiping ever since. Twenty years ago we could not have imagined where we are today. We have had many ups and downs, many challenges, but after twenty years we are still here, and our standard of worship has improved a lot from those beginning days. With your continued blessings we hope to keep improving our service to our Śacīnandana and Nitāicānda for the next twenty years, and to do our best to pass on your mercy to others.

Begging to remain attached to your lotus feet, we remain  
Your servants at ISKCON Coventry, UK.

## Denpasar

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On your most glorious appearance day, we would like to convey to you our sincere gratitude for giving us this matchless gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Through the books you have given us, the śāstric instructions you lived, and your mood of surrender, which your disciples have imbibed, we are every day learning more about ourselves and our long lost love, Śrī Krsna.

You gave us the panacea—the *mahā-mantra*—which we carry everywhere, from the famous beaches of Bali to the volcanoes that pierce the ground. We have been through a lot, and some of our devotees were even thrown in jail. They were able to tolerate the experience only because you always remained their guiding light. Your presence is felt every time we follow your instructions, and feeling such protection, we happily engage in your work.

Although you visited Jakarta only for a week during your manifested pastimes, the teachings you imparted then have continued to echo down through the years so that now thousands of devotees in this land are keeping them in their hearts.

We seek to always be of service to you and your disciples. Our desire is simply to see you pleased with our endeavors.

Your servants in Denpasar, Bali, Indonesia.

## Detroit

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you and your ever-expanding ISKCON movement!

On this most auspicious day we offer you our heartfelt gratitude for giving us everything we need to go back home, back to Godhead. Before we came in touch with you through your movement, we were totally ignorant, drowning in the ocean of material existence. You introduced us to the most merciful Lord *Caitanya*, Patita-pāvana. Your Divine Grace also gave us the prayers of great Vaiṣṇava *ācāryas* like Śrīla Narottama Dāsa Ṭhākura. In his song beginning *gaurāṅga karuṇā koro dīna hīna jane,* Śrīla Narottama Dāsa teaches us how to plead to Lord *Gaurāṅga*: “Holding grass between my teeth, O Lord Gaura, I am calling out to you now! Please be compassionate upon me and come to reside within the temple of my heart.”

Last year, 2013, will remain one of the most blessed years for us here in Detroit, for we received the blessed association of many of your dearmost leaders. Under the guidance of our beloved GBC, Her Grace Mālatī Dāsī, we started the celebration of Govardhana Pūjā with a most ecstatic *abhiṣeka* of Lord Śrī Nāthjīa celebration we will remember throughout our life. His Holiness Gopāl Kṛṣṇa Goswami and His Holiness Romapāda Swami gave us the opportunity to serve them twice during the year and enlivened us with their instructions for making progress on the path of devotional service. His Holiness Rādhānātha Swami blessed us with so much love and inspiration on his first-ever Vyāsa-pūjā celebration in Detroit. After more than twenty years since his last visit, His Holiness Jayapatāka Swami paid us a surprise visit and filled our hearts with utmost bliss.

We understand that only by your causeless mercy were we able to receive the blessed association of these beloved generals of your movement. Empowered by the blessings of these leaders, all the devotees at the Devasadan Mandir helped increase book distribution, *harināma*, our Bhakti-vṛkṣa programs, and Deity worship. In addition, we were able to upgrade the multimedia theater you envisioned so that viewers can experience devotional videos most effectively.

Last year devotees throughout Michigan blissfully attended all our festivals—Ratha-yātrā, Puṣpa-abhiṣeka, Gopāṣṭamī, etc.—in record numbers. As a result of this greater interest in the state, our leaders Śrīmān Yugala Kiśora Prabhu and Śrīmān Deva Mādhava Prabhu are working to establish centers in the suburbs of Farmington Hills and Ypsilanti. Please infuse us with the intelligence and ability to expand your movement in Michigan effectively, so we may help fulfill your desire to spread the cultural conquest of Kṛṣṇa consciousness from Detroit throughout the world.

O savior of the whole world, please continue showering your mercy on us so that we can continue serving you and our most merciful Deities, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kuñjabiharījī, until we leave these bodies. Please hold our hand tight because we have the tendency to run away from you.

Begging for forgiveness for our offenses, we remain

Your humble servants at the Devasadan Mandir in Detroit, Michigan, USA.  
(written by Jagad-Guru Dāsa)

## Durban

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

We bow down before you with gratitude.

The relationship between guru and disciple is so sacred that even the destruction of the universe cannot separate them. \[Letter from Tamāl Krishna Goswami\]

You are here, beyond doubt. Time, distance, and physical absence cannot hinder the sacred relationship you share with every devotee, on whatever level, any more than the destruction of the universe could. We can still approach you with sincerity, and you will still reciprocate.

Whatever our endeavors, it is plainly evident that what is fueling the mission of Lord *Gaurāṅga* is your powerful desire to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness to every town and village in the world. And success is coming despite all our mistakes. We remain but pieces on the chessboard. In the mood of a devotee offering Ganges water to the Ganges, we offer you our endeavors.

The instructions you imparted during your short stay in South Africa have remained the blueprint of the thriving *yātrā* we relish today. One of your main desires was that students at the universities here get the opportunity to connect to Kṛṣṇa. You wanted this even more than an exquisite temple and Ratha-yātrā. Over the last year we have been active on five campuses here, maintaining a total of nine programs weekly. Many seeking minds and hungry hearts find refuge at these programs. Every month students visit the temple, and some stay over on the weekends. Twice a semester we host student retreats on a local and national level. These retreats have changed lives.

Over the decades in South Africa, devotees of different eras have apparently been given specific missions. The first wave of devotees ploughed the field as pioneers. The second wave embraced sacrifice and labored diligently to build an inimitable temple. It appears that the mission of the third wave of devotees is to break the racial barriers, ensuring that Kṛṣṇa consciousness has a future in South Africa. The attendance at our university programs is on average comprised of 80% black students—the intelligentsia of our country. Our hopes for accomplishing the mission of the third wave largely rests with them.

These students are beginning to appreciate your books, some of which are now available in their native languages. The most recent December book marathon saw twice the number of books distributed as in the previous year. Overall, book distribution has experienced a significant and constant increase over the last few years. More amazing last December, however, was the large number of young devotees who took to the streets to share your words with their compatriots. Many had never distributed a book before. That experience affected their hearts, and the taste for sharing Kṛṣṇa with others became contagious. This was your simple formula, Śrīla Prabhupāda; you always taught us that book distribution would keep temples afloat and devotees enlivened. The marathon became a festival. And since the marathon, a team of committed distributors has kept the wheels turning.

And the Ganges water flows through our hands back into the sacred river. Even the act of worship is not to our credit. It was you who gave us the opportunity, the facility, and the motivation. We pray to become your ever-willing instruments, even if imperfect.

Your servants at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rādhānātha Temple in Durban, South Africa.

## East Hartford

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

I bow down to the beautiful lotus feet of my spiritual master, by whose causeless mercy I have received the supreme holy name,the divine mantra. \[Raghunātha Dāsa *Gosvāmī*, *Muktā-carita*\]

We want to thank you for caring about us, the fallen souls throughout the world. You were the only one who cared so much that you sacrificed your peaceful life in Vṛndāvana to accept so many problems, troubles, and anxieties just to give us the holy names. When you landed in Boston on September 18, 1965, you wrote a beautiful poem in which you expressed your humility:

I have no devotion, nor do I have any knowledge, but I have strong faith in the holy name of Kṛṣṇa.

You gave the world Lord Kṛṣṇa’s holy names, and so many people are delighted because you did. Without chanting the holy names, no one can be genuinely happy, but by chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa one becomes truly ecstatic.

We recently chanted for over six hours straight at a festival in Amherst, Massachusetts. The festival was called Extravaganja (it was a marijuana festival), and many people commented about how happy the devotees were. One person said, “They must be stoned out of their minds because they’re so happy singing the same song for six hours straight.” Actually, the mantra isn’t even a whole song—it’s just a couple of lines, and we were feeling ecstatic chanting it for hours. If it were a mundane song or a couple of lines from such a song, we would not have been able to sing it for very long. But by chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra,* which you freely gave us, we were able to chant for hours, and the longer we chanted the more enthusiastic we became, and the more ecstatically we danced.

Here in Hartford the devotees want to thank you for giving us the holy names and the blissful service of giving those holy names to others. We can’t thank you enough, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for all you have given us. Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare!

Your servants at ISKCON East Hartford, Connecticut  
(written by Pyāri Mohan Dāsa)

## Finland

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

The Finnish *yātrā* has faced some stiff challenges the last few years. The house we had rented for twenty years was sold, and we were forced to find a new place. In these modern, secular times it is very difficult for any spiritual organization to find proper accommodations. The real estate business is such that it was almost impossible to find a place to accommodate all temple activities, the Bhaktivedanta College, and living facilities. When we would find a suitable place, the cost was far beyond our capacity.

Still, in this helpless situation we made a full effort and didn’t give up. ISKCON became famous all around Finland because of our hard struggle to get a proper place. When we first began our search we faced hardcore political and racist elements that aimed to prevent ISKCON from getting any property whatsoever. These elements’ illegal and dishonest acts raised a big countermovement that worked to our advantage. The media and many politicians, scholars, lawyers, Hindu organizations, the Indian ambassador to Finland, and even the Lutheran Church (Protestants) stepped forward to support ISKCON. We enjoyed vast positive publicity all over Finland. This showed us clearly the good results of our preaching here for many decades.

To top it all off, the City of Helsinki offered us a very nice big manor house in the center of town. ISKCON Finland is now settled down and operating in that government-protected old manor in the middle of Helsinki. The place fulfills all our needs: it’s perfect for temple activities, congregational preaching, educational functions, and *āśrama* development. Not only are the devotees happy, but people in general are now positively aware of ISKCON and its achievements in many different areas.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, these last few years have been a great opportunity for us to perform *saṅkīrtana* on a wider scale than ever before. They have shown us what is possible when all *āśramas* and *varṇas* work in unison. The Manor project not only showed that we have more supporters than we ever thought, but it also made it clear how strong the power of your instructions is and how your movement is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants at ISKCON Finland.  
(written by Tattvavāda Dāsa)

## Gainesville

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Mahātmā

*Mahātmās* are broad-minded souls who always see the best.  
They tolerate and liberate those others would reject.  
*Because externals aren*’t eternal, they dismiss the dress  
and help the soul within begin to truly manifest.  
My dear, beloved Master, for a span of forty years,  
*you offered kṛṣṇa-bhakti* but found few receptive ears.  
*And then Lord* Kṛṣṇa took you and astonished all your peers  
by sailing you unaided to the backside of this sphere.  
*If you*’d been narrow-minded, would you sail behind the earth  
to look for lost potential where the others saw no worth?  
Retrieving and believing in us, firmly, yet with mirth,  
you planted us in fertile lands so ISKCON could take birth.  
You did not curse our craziness, our decadence and flaws;  
*you won our hearts completely*, giving love that had no cause.  
You took our best, our fight and drive, our will to scratch and claw,  
*and sent us out on saṅkīrtana,* though we were young and raw.  
The drumbeat of critiquing us began that very day,  
*for everybody honored you, but we were bad, they*’d say.  
Mistakes we made were epic, and some leaders went astray,  
but in our hearts we were too smart to let you get away.  
Now as our lives are ending and we look back at the trail,  
*although it hasn*’t gone too far, the train’s still on the rails.  
And I recall that frenzied fall when your health finally failed.  
You only wanted us with you, and still your love prevails.  
*Now what will come tomorrow*, only Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa knows.  
*When East and West don*’t neatly mesh, it keeps us on our toes.  
*Though cripple-minded kṛpaṇas* still can’t see past their nose,  
because you loved us, one and all, your congregation grows.  
*You are the true* Mahātmā, Prabhupād, for what you’ve done  
*is possible because you see all souls as* Kṛṣṇa’s sons.  
The quality of mercy is not strained, but gently comes  
as tears of separation when your words adorn our tongues.

Your servants at ISKCON Gainesville, Florida, USA.  
(written by Kālakaṇṭha dasa)

## Genova

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*I bow down with full respect and love at the dust of your most glorious and eminent lotus feet! Bando sāvadhāna mate*.

Today being the auspicious appearance day of Śrīpād Śrīla Rāmānujācārya, I feel very inspired to write about you.

As the potent *sampradāya*-*ācārya* Śrī Rāmānuja challenged and defeated the impersonalists, so you challenged and defeated all our misconceptions and transformed our lives with your strong and merciful preaching. Thanks to you, today Śrī Kṛṣṇa is known everywhere. Wherever we go, people welcome us as the Hare Kṛṣṇa devotees. Your missionary spirit has no limit. At the age of 69, your health very precarious, with no economic security and no assistants, you traveled to America alone, depending only on your books, your Guru Maharaja’s order, and the mercy of Śrī Kṛṣṇa. What an example for all of us! We shouldn’t worry about our old age, our security, our pension, and so on. We should consider the order of Caitanya Mahāprabhu the highest priority: *yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa*. We should preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness to everyone we meet. Old age is approaching all of us; so let’s make the order and the example of our dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda the topmost priority in our lives.

This year in Italy we are celebrating the fortieth anniversary of your visit to our country. In 1974, at the end of May, you visited our small temple in Rome. Forty years have passed, and now is the time to renew our vow to purify our lives more and more and dedicate ourselves to your mission—spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world for the benefit of all conditioned souls.

Let’s work together as humble representatives of Your Divine Grace! *Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda, our divine lord and master! Please allow us to remain forever among your serious and faithful servants.

With love and deep respect,  
Your servants in Genova, Italy.  
(written by Dayānidhi Dāsa)

## Gianyar

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, the real messenger of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura!

The radiance of your books has destroyed the proud philosophies of *nirviśeṣa* and *śūnyavādī.* You changed the face and consciousness of the world by distributing love of Godhead to everyone equally.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you established the *saṅkīrtana* movement and dozens of temples, thus fulfilling the prediction of Lord *Gaurāṅga that His name world be heard in every town and village. The* history of the world will record your achievement. The world is eternally indebted to you. You live eternally in the hearts of your followers.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray for your kind mercy. May we fallen servants of your servants eternally remain so.

The servants of your servants at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava Mandira in Gianyar, Bali, Indonesia.  
(written by Dhīra Dāsa and Kiśora Kṛṣṇa Dāsa)

## Gītā-nāgarī

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances at the dust of your transcendental lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace on this most auspicious day of your appearance!

As one by one your disciples and granddisciples leave to join you in the next great adventure, your legacy for *Gītā*-nāgarī becomes more pertinent. The vision you gave us, of a self-sufficient agriculture-based devotional community, which you said was “An extremely important part of our Movement,” (letter to Rūpānuga, 18 December 1974), is manifesting day by day by your causeless mercy. *Gītā*-nāgarī now has the only Grade “A” certified slaughter-free dairy in the USA, serving the devotee communities of New York, New Jersey, Potomac, Baltimore, Philadelphia, Delaware, Detroit, and Harrisburg.
