Śrīla Prabhupāda, since you are our preeminent instructing spiritual master, we realize Your Divine Grace to be a bona fide representative of Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu Himself teaches us from within and without as the instructing guru. He teaches us within as the Supersoul, Paramātmā, our constant companion, and from without He teaches us from the *Bhagavad-gītā* as the instructing spiritual master. As the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are authorized by Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu to deliver Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you enrich all your *dīkṣā* and *śikṣā* disciples with full spiritual knowledge, which awakens them to the activities of devotional service.

By learning from Your Divine Grace, a truly self-realized spiritual master, how to engage in the service of Lord Viṣṇu, a disciple begins functional devotional service. Śrīla Prabhupāda, some unfortunately do not understand the following transcendental facts: For the thousands of disciples for whom you are the initiating spiritual master, Your Divine Grace is a personal manifestation of both Śrīla Madana-mohana *vigraha* and Śrīla Govindadeva *vigraha*, and for your many thousands of *śikṣā disciples* Your Divine Grace is a personal manifestation of Śrīla Govindadeva *vigraha*. (This we learn from your purport to *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi* 1.47)

*ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyān nāvamanyeta karhicit*  
*na martya-buddhyāsūyeta sarva-deva-mayo guruḥ*

**“One should know the** ***ācārya*** **as Myself and never disrespect him in any way. One should not envy him, thinking him an ordinary man, for he is the representative of all the demigods.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **11.17.27)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON, thirty-second in disciplic line from Kṛṣṇa in the *Gauḍīya* *sampradāya*, Your Divine Grace is always offering blessings to everyone fortunate enough to contact you or appreciate you, what to speak of sincerely serve you. An ever-increasing worldwide congregation of new, aspiring, and dedicated practitioners are arriving on the path of devotion leading to the true and pure, unadulterated platform of *kṛṣṇa*-*bhakti*. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the beloved *dīkṣā-guru* of thousands of wonderful, beautiful, and great disciples. Your Divine Grace is the preeminent *śikṣā-guru* for those thousands of fortunate and blessed *dīkṣā* disciples, and you are the preeminent *śikṣā*-*guru* for thousands of thousands of younger *kṛṣṇa*-*bhaktas*. Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, Your Divine Grace has the potential to be the preeminent *śikṣā-guru* for millions, and even millions of millions. Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, someday you may have billions and trillions of *śikṣā* disciples. Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are like the great Śrīla Nārada Muni or the great Śrī Prahlāda Mahārāja. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are qualified to be the *śikṣā-guru* for everyone in the universe.

Such is the great legacy of a great moonlike pure devotee of Gaura-Nitāi and Rādhā- Govindadeva, such is the great legacy of a moonlike surrendered *mahātmā* disciple of the great ray of Viṣṇu Śrīla *Bhaktisiddhānta* Sarasvatī *Gosvāmī* Mahārāja, and such is the great legacy of the ever well-wisher, the kindest friend, and the great spiritual master for a multitude of unfortunate, misguided, and misdirected spirit souls who hanker for the peace, mercy, blessings, and love of complete Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Yes, this is your great and wonderful legacy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and therefore we are humbled and feel most insignificant assembled at your lotus feet.

We, Śrīla Prabhupāda, your most fortunate followers, feel incomplete, imperfect. Yes, we feel fallible, Śrīla Prabhupāda, although we are standing in the direct line of the causeless shower of your mercy and perfect, infallible Kṛṣṇa conscious guidance. How can this be, Śrīla Prabhupāda? We hear from Your Divine Grace the real true Kṛṣṇa conscious *siddhānta,* we pray, we worship, and we chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, *Jaya* Nitāi-*Gaurāṅga*, and *Jaya* Rādhe Govinda together, Śrīla Prabhupāda, which surely brings us together in faith, purpose, and dedication. Yet somehow or other, after the festival ends and we go our separate ways, all too often we become a little less fortunate—why *less* fortunate? sometimes *un*fortunate—and we lapse into forgetfulness of the great urgency, need, and hope of our mission, of your mission, Śrīla Prabhupāda, of the great Vaiṣṇava *ācāryas’* mission, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and especially of the great mission of the Golden Avatāra, Śrī Gaurāṅga, Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and of Śrī Nityānanda Balarāma, Śrī Advaita Ācārya, Śrī Gadādhara Prabhu, and Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura.

Please bless us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, with a drop of special all-powerful spiritual potency so we no longer lapse into such unfortunate forgetfulness, so we no longer lapse into spiritual weakness, so we no longer risk wasting this great opportunity for achieving complete Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this lifetime, in this precious human form of life.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, bless us, your insignificant followers, with just a drop of awesome spiritual potency so we will perfectly obey and serve the higher order of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and the Parampāda Bhagavān Śrīla Govindadeva as He wisely directs all humanity with these supreme words:

*yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa*  
*āmāra ājñāya guru hañā tāra’ ei deśa*

***“*****Instruct everyone to follow the orders of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa as they are given in the** ***Bhagavad-gītā*** **and** ***Śrīmad*****-*****Bhāgavatam*****. In this way become a spiritual master and try to liberate everyone in this land.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya*** **7.128)**

*na rūpam asyeha tathopalabhyate*  
*nānto na cādir na ca sampratiṣṭhā*  
*aśvattham enaṁ su-virūḍha-mūlam*  
*asaṅga-śastreṇa dṛḍhena chittvā*

*tataḥ pādaṁ tat parimārgitavyaṁ*  
*yasmin gatā na nivartanti bhūyaḥ*  
*tam eva cādyaṁ puruṣaṁ prapadye*  
*yataḥ pravṛttiḥ prasṛtā purāṇī*

**“The real form of this tree cannot be perceived in this world. No one can understand where it ends, where it begins, or where its foundation is. But with determination one must cut down thisstrongly rooted tree with the weapon of detachment. Thereafter, one must seek that place from which, having gone, one never returns, and there surrender to that Supreme Personality of Godhead from whom everything began and from whom everything has extended since time immemorial.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **15.3–4)**

This is the sublime mission of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please never kick us away. All of us love you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

We beg to remain your servants forever.

Your unfortunate, insignificant servants at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govindadeva Mandira, Brooklyn, New York, New York, USA.  
(written by Rāmabhadra Dāsa)

## Noida

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble and respectful obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet! All glories to your Vyāsa-pūjā!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā is always full of joy and excitement for all your children and grandchildren. On the morning after the busy night of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Janmāṣṭamī, when everyone would prefer to take some extra rest, your sincere followers look forward to preparing for and taking part in the celebration of your Vyāsa-pūjā festival. This is the sweet relationship you have developed with all the devotees trained and nurtured in ISKCON.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are grateful to you for millions of things, particularly for giving us wonderful temples in all the cities and towns on the surface of earth, a perfect arrangement for associating with devotees of Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, with your blessings we were able to inaugurate the Noida temple this year on *Nityānanda* Trayodaśī, as per the commitment we made to you on your Vyāsa-pūjā last year. This could happen only due to your causeless mercy and the guidance and inspiration from your dear disciple His Holiness Lokanāth Swami, who happily went through a lot of spiritual anxiety for this prestigious project solely for the pleasure of Your Divine Grace. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we took a little longer than expected to complete this project due to our incompetence, but as the saying goes, “All’s well that ends well.”

We had a memorable inauguration festival, attended by many devotees from different parts of the world who came to bless this function, justifying the name you gave this revolutionary society: the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. In addition, thousands of guests attended the opening festival, and everyone was served nice *prasādam* on all three days. Everyone witnessed the rare *mahā*-*abhiṣeka* and *prāṇa-pratiṣṭhā* ceremonies for the Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govindadeva, Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā, and Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we could feel your presence in every sphere of the festival; it was actually Your Divine Grace who was making things happen here.

On this special day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we beg at your lotus feet to empower us with wisdom and purity so that we can utilize the nice facilities you’ve kindly provided us at Noida for distributing the holy name of the Lord to each and every living entity living in every street and home of this city. Please bless us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, so that we can get back to our preaching services with renewed vigor and improve the condition of the lives of everyone here.

Thank you once again, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for everything you have done for all of us.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Vyāsa-pūjā mahotsava kī jaya!

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your insignificant servants at ISKCON Noida, India.

## Pandharpur

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

We owe you an infinite debt of gratitude for the divine knowledge you have given us. Through your books you have given us the ability to see the world with transcendental vision, and you have explained *bhagavat-tattva*-*vijñāna—*the science of Godhead—to all of us. By opening temples worldwide, you gave everyone the opportunity to regularly hear *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, *Bhagavad-gītā,* etc., and also an opportunity to take *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*.

This year we arranged the program of Paṇḍharpūra-Dhāma-Pañcakrośī-Parikramā. During these three days of *parikramā,* more than a hundred devotees from different parts of Maharashtra participated. Accompanied by the Deities of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, devotees circumambulated Pandharpur *dhāma* while chanting and dancing enthusiastically. People in the villages on the *parikramā* path welcomed the devotees, and there was *kṛṣṇa-kathā,* *kīrtana,* and grand book and *prasādam* distribution.

This year we also celebrated the 500th anniversary of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s visit to Maharashtra and Pandharpur *dhāma* when He was on His South India tour. On this occasion we arranged a *padayātrā* from Kolhapura to Nasik, following the path that Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu traveled. *Padayātrā* devotees walked the following path: Kolhapura-Arawade-Pandharpur-Satara (Krishna-Venna Sangam)-Pune-Mumbai-Wada-Nasik.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you greatly emphasized cow protection. The *gośālā* at ISKCON Pandharpur is flourishing. The number of cows and bulls are increasing. Milk has also increased. Devotees are very happy to drink milk from our own cows. Cow dung obtained from the *gośālā* is being properly utilized on the farm as manure. We are trying our best to keep the cows happy. Kindly guide us to develop this cow protection program, which is very dear Śrī Govinda and you.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda! You are the commander in chief of Śrī *Gaurāṅga* Mahāprabhu’s army. We are following your commands by distributing your transcendental books, and we are traveling to many towns and villages to distribute the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*.

The Śrīla Prabhupāda Ghāṭa Project we started last year is now sixty percent completed. We are going to offer this project to Your Divine Grace on the auspicious occasion of ISKCON’s Golden Jubilee (50th anniversary) in 2016.

Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us a chance to serve you in various ways. We beg you to kindly keep us engaged in your divine service eternally.

Your servants at ISKCON Pandharpur, India.

## Paris

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prahbupāda,

On behalf of the devotees in the Paris temple, I will attempt to write something for your glorification on your auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day.

I ask myself, What is it that makes you the most important person not only in my life but factually in the lives of every human being on the planet, although most of them do not know it yet.

A concise answer: You gave us a way of life that enables anyone who follows it to transcend the miserable material existence altogether and attain the shelter of Kṛṣṇa in the spiritual world.

That is not something esoteric but is the most tangible thing there is. It is the fulfillment of our innermost desires, the solution to all our problems. Who could give us all that except for you?

Many wise men have come and gone throughout history, and their thoughts continue to inspire and enlighten, but you are not just one of them. You gave us the complete package: everything we need to make a succes out of this otherwise wretched life. Your gifts of course include your books, the law books for the next ten thousand years, and the mentors you personally trained in this science and empowered to train the next generation in the same very pratical way.

How can I express myself with such confidence? Because I have seen your instructions work wonders in my own life. By my following the path you outlined, my life has changed from night to day. When I think back and try to remember what my life was before you saved me some thirty years ago, I can only compare it to a nightmare of the most horrific sort. It is truly a miracle that you have effected this change.

While writing these words I am sitting in Śrīdhām Māyāpur, Lord Caitanya’s transcendental abode. Through my doorway I have a stunning view of the rising Temple of the Vedic Planetarium, the Vatican of Lord Caitanya’s Golden Age. In the middle of the rice fields of one of India’s poorest, most remote areas, this magnificent edifice is becoming manifest. How transcendental! And it is all because of you, and you alone. The many other temples in the area are hardly noticeable next to the massive temple complex you are creating here through your dedicated followers, whose hearts reverbrate day and night with the fervent instructions you imparted to them.

The special quality you have that distinguishes you from everyone else, even many saintly persons, is your absolutely firm faith in the instructions of your spiritual master and the holy name of the Lord. Even above that, one might say, is your unlimited compassion for the conditioned souls suffering in this world. You gave us something we had never known before, namely, unconditional, unselfish love—a love that the whole world is thirsting after, a love that makes life worth living, a love that makes hell into heaven, a love whose absence makes heaven into hell.

One of your disciples, in great amazement, once told you: “Śrīla Prabhupāda, everyone likes you so much.” Your response really revealed your secret: “Yes, because I like everyone.”

Recently I have relocated to ISKCON Paris. Here I feel closer to you in the sense that France in general and Paris specifically was a place of many pastimes of yours, and also because there are so many of your direct disciples here. You of course also had to face some strong opposition here, maybe even more than in other countries, since France has a tradition of revolution and anarchy, especially among the younger generation.

In the face of such opposition, you showed even more greatness. Simultaneously humble and determined, you persevered in all circumstances. When the students demanded “*par terre*” (“Get down on the floor!”), you humbly asked that for public appearances you be given an ordinary chair instead of the opulent *vyāsāsana* your followers had offered you. On the other hand, when your comparison of Kṛṣṇa to a president of a country whom we have to obey evoked loud protests, you didn’t budge but repeated the example, even causing the assembly to erupt to such a degree that you had to end the lecture.

Most famous is of course your conversation with Cardinal Danilou in Paris. You uncomprmisingly exposed his hypocrisy when he suggested that it was ok to kill cows to feed hungry children. He was obviously intent on maintaining a cordial mood, but you refused to submit to his sentiment if it meant compromising the truth. Let us similarly serve you here in France as warriors fighting for the Absolute Truth. Your satisfaction is our sustenance, our only real objective, and the very perfection of our lives.

Your humble servants in Paris, France.  
(written by Gaurahari Dāsa)

## Perth

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.  
Your glories, your books,  
We’ve not mistook.  
So much information that’s relieving,  
So many people receiving,  
Coming down through the ages,  
And now from you, the best of sages.  
Applying your preaching with determination,  
for you we give such admiration.  
It’s after a heck of a search  
we’ve come across this brethren church.  
It’s brown ’n’ ugly to see from outside,  
but now kṛṣṇa-kīrtan is sung in stride.  
Your ISKCON’s a nice place to chant  
if we can tolerate the neighbors’ rant.  
They’re bringing complaint of noise,  
but people are coming with ease and poise.  
It’s your GBC who’s said this state is so bleak.  
We’re happy to have your devotees come and speak.

Your servants at ISKCON Perth, Australia.

## Phoenix

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you, the general of the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya Mahaprabhu! All glories to you! All glories to you!

As we sit down to write this offering, we look back and reflect on the activities of the past few months. We cannot write empty flowery words, but we write to you from our heart because we know that you will get the message.

Like many of your spiritual centers, we have had our ups and downs, some challenging moments, but at the end we see you sitting on the *vyāsāsana* and are confident that you are the captain of our ship. We believe with all our hearts that if we follow your instructions and sincerely surrender to Lord Kṛṣṇa, everything will run the way He wants it to run.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the source of our inspiration to serve Their Lordships, your instructions are the nectar we relish to feed our minds, and you are the guiding light on our way back to Godhead, in our quest for devotional service. Here we are, *Śrīla Prabhupāda*! Here we are! Please do not leave us. Please help us understand and follow your instructions. Please help us stay away from false ego, from negativity. Please help us develop love, tolerance, humility, and faith. Please help us fulfill your desire and make of ISKCON a great spiritual embassy. Please help us become devotees and achieve *kṛṣṇa-prema*.

Your servants at ISKCON Phoenix, Arizona, USA.

## Phoenix (Mauritius)

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to your ISKCON!

You once predicted that Mauritius would be the first Kṛṣṇa conscious country, and so that will certainly happen, but we do not know how or when. Various devotees here are implementing various preaching strategies, but at the same time Māyās attacks are becoming more and more fierce and threatening. Book distribution, *harināma,* Deity worship, festivals, and Food for Life programs are all growing, but side by side so are the attacks on your ISKCON.

Your disciples and granddisciples are doing everything possible to protect your ISKCON, and we desperately need your mercy to achieve that goal. Alone you established and protected ISKCON from so many attacks. We can’t imitate you but can only pray to be empowered to follow in your footsteps.

Big preaching means big enemies. Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu had to face Jagāi and Mādhāi. Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura had to face the entrenched British culture and the *sahajiyās*. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura had to face a variety of opponents. And Your Divine Grace also had to face various obstacles, like the difficulty acquiring the Juhu land, the Price affair, and the dacoits in Māyāpur.

On this most auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, we humbly pray that you guide us from within to protect your ISKCON from so many attacks.

You gave so many specific instructions for Mauritius during your visit in 1975. Alas! If only we had listened and implemented those instructions. But we are confident that your desire and prediction will surely one day come to fruition.

Begging to serve your ISKCON eternally under your representatives, we remain

Your unworthy servants in Pheonix, Mauritius.  
(written by Ajay Caitanya Dāsa)

## Prague

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Please accept our respectful obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

This auspicious holy day of your Vyāsa-pūjā is always an opportunity to reflect on your mercy and the sacrifices you underwent to give us ignorant Westerners Kṛṣṇa consciousness. On this day we try to deepen our appreciation of your mercy and thus rededicate our bodies, minds, and words to you.

In a letter you wrote:

Actually these books are the foundation stone of our movement. Whatever we are is resting on these books, so far reading them and distributing them. This should be our only motto. \[Letter to Hridayānanda, 31 October 1974\]

Yes, *Śrīla Prabhupāda*, your most important gift to us certainly consists of your books, which you so painstakingly translated in the early morning hours, renouncing sleep. You wrote them with the intention that they would be the law books for mankind for the next 10,000 years—you gave them so much importance. And you yourself personally read them and distributed them. You told us that they were actually dictated by Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself.

You said that every verse and purport in the *Śrīmad*-*Bhāgavatam*—indeed, every word!—is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa. The *Bhāgavatam* is all transcendental; it is *apauruṣeya;* it comes from outside this material world, from Kṛṣṇa in the spiritual world. The *Bhāgavatam* is meant for creating a revolution in the misled materialistic society.

From the pages of your books comes the transcendental vision (*śāstra*-*cakṣus*) of different realities: our fallen condition, the spiritual world, our relationship with Kṛṣṇa. From your books we get the inspiration to serve Kṛṣṇa and return to Him. From practicing the *sādhana,* studying your books, chanting the holy name, and performing *saṅkīrtana* we get even more inspiration and realization that all this is actually truth, because it works. And then we are inspired to give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the suffering people of the world in a way they are able to understand.

We will try our best to increase book distribution in this *yātrā,* and we humbly pray to you for help and blessings so that this endeavor will be successful. If you wish, please send us some new devotees to help. Thank you very much.

Saṅkīrtana-yajña kī jaya! Śrīla Prabhupāda kī jaya!

Your servants at Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Navadvīpacandra Mandira, Prague, the Czech Republic.

## Radhadesh

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We, the residents of Radhadesh-dhāma, beg to offer our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. Although it is not possible to describe the value of what you have bestowed upon us, we nevertheless take the opportunity to offer a palmful of words enumerating your glories into the ocean of your glories, in an attempt to indicate our eternal indebtedness to Your Divine Grace.

So many souls come to visit Radhadesh, and so many comment on how the atmosphere here is full of peace and happiness. When we reflect on what it is that makes Radhadesh such a haven, we can consider its setting—the charming and gently sloping Ardennes that display a great variety of natural beauty in the form of woodlands, meadows, and forest walks. There is the *gośālā,* with its happy cows and oxen, the thriving permaculture gardens growing ample vegetables and flowers, the guesthouse, restaurant, boutique, bakery, conference halls, Bhaktivedanta College, Bhaktivedanta Library Services, the sacred-art museums, the playgrounds where children frolic, and the happy, friendly devotees going to and fro while performing a variety of services.

But all these wonders emanate from and circumambulate the castle, which contains the cynosure of the entire property: the temple room, where reside Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha, Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā, and Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitai, and Your Divine Grace. Even visitors who may have some difficulty comprehending the significance of Their Lordships are awed when they look at your *mūrti,* and it is not unusual for them to whisper, “How does he manage to sit so still?” When they hear about your unprecedented sacrifice and glorious victory in spreading the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement worldwide, all in the last twelve years of your manifest *līlā,* they are deeply impressed by your unique selflessness and devotion. They then begin to realize who is behind this array of exotic art and devotional culture, maintained by so many content and amiable devotees. Seeing their responses to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we recall your words in the purport of *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* chapter 9, text 29:

When a diamond is set in a golden ring, it looks very nice. The gold is glorified, and at the same time the diamond is glorified. The Lord and the living entity eternally glitter, and when a living entity becomes inclined to the service of the Supreme Lord he looks like gold. The Lord is a diamond, and so this combination is very nice.

We know, of course, that there is no question of your having “become inclined” to the service of the Lord. You mercifully appeared in this world according to Kṛṣṇa’s request, just to offer the greatest benediction to any fortunate soul who could even dimly recognize its value.

We who have the great fortune of living in the shelter of all you have provided pray to never take such perfect facilities for granted, but to utilize them to satisfy you, who desire only the satisfaction of the Lord. You know perfectly well that His satisfaction is the source of unending peace and happiness. You, who are beyond the all-pervading envy of the material influence, would have us become like you—a golden ring to glorify the diamond Lord. Obedience to your instructions is our access to genuine happiness and the ability to guide others who desire authentic spiritual life.

On this 118th anniversary of your appearance, we are so grateful to still be counted among your followers and servants. May we be blessed to always stay sheltered in the company of those who know you as our eternal guide linking us to the Lord and His entourage.

Always aspiring to be worthy servants of your servants, we remain  
The Radhadesh-vāsīs, in Belgium.

## Recife, Brazil.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most important and auspicious day in my life, your appearance day, I will try to express some words of gratitude.

I feel gratitude because you came to the West to show your love for us fallen souls.

I feel gratitude because you distributed your mercy to all, regardless of our (my) fallen condition.

I feel gratitude because you wrote so many books, revealing the most valuable treasure—Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s holy name, activities, glories, etc.

I feel gratitude because you made it easy for us to follow you and serve you by helping you in your mission.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I could keep writing about my feelings of gratitude toward you, but there is not enough space. That’s why I am trying to show my gratitude to you by being a good servant. *Śrīla Prabhupāda*, I aspire to be your devotee eternally.

Thank you once more, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your fallen servants in Recife, Brazil.  
(written by Jagad-bhartā Dāsa)

## Richmond

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you! All glories to this wonderful festival of Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā!

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja *Gosvāmī* describes Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s tour of South India in these words:

As soon as \[Gaurahari\] saw someone, He would request him to chant “Hari! Hari!” Whoever heard Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu chant “Hari! Hari!” also chanted the holy name of Lord Hari and Kṛṣṇa. Each of these empowered persons would return to his own village, always chanting the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Such an empowered person would request everyone and anyone—whomever he saw—to chant the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. In this way all the villagers would also become devotees of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. People from different villages who came to see such an empowered individual would become like him simply by seeing him and receiving the mercy of his glance. When each of these newly empowered individuals returned to his own village, he also converted the villagers into devotees. And when others came from different villages to see him, they were also converted. In this way, as empowered men went from one village to another, all the people of South India became devotees. (*Madhya* 7.97–104).

Śrīla Prabhupāda, this is the story of ISKCON. You brought Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mercy to the world, and you empowered your disciples to carry His message to every town and village. And when people come in contact with your dedicated disciples, they too have been inspired to become carriers of Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mercy. In this way, the contagion of Kṛṣṇa consciousness continues to spread far and wide.

And this is also the story of ISKCON Richmond. Our little ISKCON center here, in the capital of Virginia, has existed for over a decade now, passed from the hands of one devotee to another. No one was officially charged with starting a center in Richmond. Rather, each devotee who ran it was inspired to serve you in this way, and when that devotee had to move on due to work or family, the devotee made sure to leave it in the hands of another. And throughout these years, we have received guidance from your empowered disciples. Today, for the first time, we have the privilege of making a formal offering to you on your Vyāsa-pūjā day.

This year we were legally incorporated as the International Society for Krishna Consciousness of Richmond, a nonprofit organization—the newest member of your family of centers in the United States. We had a record year for book distribution—nearly five hundred books were sold during the December marathon alone. And we welcomed five devotees into your family: Mathurādhīśa Dāsa and Navīna-Rādhā Devī Dāsī received initiation from His Holiness Bhakti Cāru Swami; Subala-sakha Dāsa and Kṛṣṇa-pramodinī Devī Dāsī were initiated by His Holiness Hanumatpresaka Swami; and Rādhikā-rati Devī Dāsī received shelter from His Holiness *Rādhā*nātha Swami. Several other couples are chanting sixteen rounds of the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra daily and worshiping Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi at home. The youth of ISKCON Richmond have started a Bhakti Yoga Club at Virginia Commonwealth University, where we have hosted *harināmas,* book tables, and a Sacred Sounds *kīrtana* festival. Throughout all these activities we have tried our best to maintain a spirit of cooperation in your service, for we are mindful of this instruction of yours: “Your love for me will be shown by how you cooperate among yourselves after I am gone.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, here in Richmond we are weak and unstable in our Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Sometimes we are overzealous, at other times lethargic. At times we lack confidence, and at other times we become proud. But one thing we know for sure: we rely one hundred percent on your mercy for our survival. Please protect our sprouting *bhakti* creeper and guide our future development, both individual and organizational.

We end with two verses written about you by Prof. M. Narasimhachary, head of the department of Vaishnavism at the University of Madras, who passed away last year. He wrote a hundred beautiful Sanskrit verses glorifying you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and here are two of them:

*śrī-kṛṣṇa-caitanya-mahā-prabhūṇāṁ*  
*kāruṇya-pīyūṣa-mahā-pravāhaḥ*  
*ekatra loke puruṣe ’vatīrṇaḥ*  
*sa eva cāsmad-guru-sārvabhaumaḥ*

**“The great river of the nectar of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mercy has descended to earth in one person. That is our Śrīla Prabhupāda, the emperor among spiritual masters.”**

*śrī-rādhikā-vallabha-mandirāṇaṁ*  
*nirmāṇam āsīn na paraṁ dharitryām*  
*sad-bhakta-citteṣv api citram etat*  
*gurūttamena pratibhā-yutena*

**“With great intuition, the best among spiritual masters, Śrīla Prabhupāda, constructed temples of Lord Kṛṣṇa, the beloved of Śrī Rādhikā, all over the world. At the same time he established these temples in the hearts of honest devotees. How wonderful this is!”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for your books, thank you for ISKCON, and thank you for your empowered disciples.

Your grandchildren in Richmond, Virginia, USA.  
(written by Rādhikā Ramaṇa Dāsa)

## Richmond Hill

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

How can we really appreciate all that you have done?  
Because of you the spiritual revolution has begun.  
When we think of what would have happened if you had not come,  
Our hearts and minds become weak and numb.  
Because of you we have been saved;  
Because of you tens of thousands of devotees have been made.  
We were floating in the ocean of Māyās grip,  
Unknowingly holding on to a sinking ship.  
We were chasing this life of illusion;  
But you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, gave us the real solution.  
Like Lord Nityānanda, you came to save countless Jagāis and Mādhāis;  
You are our savior, sent directly from the spiritual skies.  
You gave us a taste of this sweet nectar of bhakti,  
Requesting that we share this openly and freely.  
You knew that all of civilization is in a precarious position,  
Everyone living their lives in sinful sense gratification.  
Your appearance was predicted well before your arrival:  
Lord Caitanya’s saṅkīrtana movement would see a revival.  
Indeed, in the year 1896 you did appear  
Right after Janmāṣṭamī: Abhay Charan—one without fear.  
You would be taught in Vaiṣṇavism right from the start,  
From worship of the Deities to pulling a Ratha-yātrā cart.  
In 1922 you would meet Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākur;  
This exalted soul of the unbroken paramparā would become your guru.  
In that meeting you were given your life’s mission:  
Go out and preach Lord Caitanya’s message across the nations.  
After all, you were Lord Kṛṣṇa’s dearmost representative;  
You were the one that was indeed predicted.  
Honored with the title of Bhaktivedanta,  
You gave up all material connections by taking sannyāsa.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, with your determination,  
You would preach the process of self-realization.  
Following the instructions of your spiritual master,  
You would translate *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, chapter after chapter.  
Across the ocean you would travel on the Jaladuta,  
Armed with volumes of books and purity you arrived in America.  
Despite many struggles, you came as Lord Kṛṣṇa’s ambassador,  
Depending on Him and doing all for His pleasure.  
From the first public kīrtan in Tompkins Square Park,  
You would begin to dispel those in the dark.  
Your thousands of awe-inspiring lectures, letters, books and festivals  
Were topped by hundreds of interviews, kīrtans, trips, and temples.  
The topmost Vedic philosophy would be found in your ISKCON,  
Something that could easily be practiced dawn to dusk to dawn.  
The Hare Kṛṣṇa mahā-mantra would resound in every town and city,  
Providing the opportunity for relief for all humanity.  
Preaching is the essence; books are the basis.  
Each and every word of your books so perfectly translated.  
Book distribution you would so strongly emphasize;  
This effective mṛdaṅga beat you would ensure we recognize.  
Based on a bona fide paramparā, you built this foundation;  
Your request was that it be continued in cooperation.  
With these instructions we could go back home, back to Godhead;  
Nothing else is required, you yourself said.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are so very fortunate:  
In our lifetime we were able to meet someone so great.  
You gave us our true purpose in life:  
“Get out of the clutches of māyā and its strife.”  
Your Divine Grace is most merciful—  
So much kindness you bestowed on our unworthy souls.  
Undoubtedly, there is no way to express our gratitude,  
All we can say is “Thank you, thank you, thank you” in servitude.  
We bow down again and again at your lotus feet;  
Please insure that any distractions to our service end in defeat.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, please continue to shower us with your blessings.  
Please help us escape this age of quarrel and hypocrisy by all means.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda! Jaya Gurudeva!

Your humble servants in Richmond Hill, Canada.  
(written by Mīṇākṣī Devī Dāsī)

## Rome

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances unto your lotus feet.

In your *Kṛṣṇa* book, while commenting on the prayers of the personified *Vedas,* you explain how the pure devotee is electrified by Kṛṣṇa consciousness and how he is able to electrify his disciples, too.

The devotees who were blessed with your direct association, both men and women, personally imbibed the intensity of your dedication to your Guru Mahārāja, of your desire to spread the holy name, of your desire to have your books distributed, and of your stalwart determination to open temples in every city of the world.

Two such devotees are Dhanañjaya Prabhu and his wife Bāla Gopāla *Mātājī*, who, electrified directly by you, in 1973 decided to open the first Kṛṣṇa temple in Rome. You blessed their good idea and promised them that as soon as they found a fit place, you would join them.

The temple this devoted couple inaugurated in Rome was located in a very congested area, and when you arrived there in May of 1974, they apologized to you for the traffic din. Much to their surprise, you appreciated the noise as a sign that the temple was situated in a central and strategic preaching area.

Just a few days after you had introduced the *guru*-*pūjā* song in the Bombay temple, you introduced it for the first time in the West in the newly inaugurated Rome temple and taught the devotees there how to sing it.

During your visit to Rome you remarked on the religious nature of the Italians, calling them “pious people,” and when the devotees wished to show you the Coliseum, you remarked how by the action of time even great empires like the Roman Empire crumble and their majestic structures become nothing but homes for stray cats.

When you asked the devotees if there was a toilet in the Coliseum and they replied that there was not, you said that the Coliseum was a monumental useless giant.

Forty years have passed since your visit to Rome, and on this special day we wish to show you how much your spiritual power keeps flowing unceasingly through your instructions, your sincere representatives, and your ISKCON, “electrifying” whoever comes in contact with it.

We are hoping that in time this prominent metropolis will showcase all the wonderful activities of your worldwide ISKCON movement, starting with the first-ever Rome Ratha-yātrā festival, to be held next May in the center of the city.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we beg you to bless us so that we may stay fixed in devotional service, in the company of other devotees, enlivened by an enthusiastic spirit of cooperation.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for all you have given us and are still giving us.

With everlasting gratitude, we remain  
*Y*our humble, aspiring servants in Rome, Italy.

## San Antonio

Our dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you!

On behalf of the devotees here in San Antonio, we would like to offer this humble, heartfelt homage at your lotus feet as a small token of our infinite gratitude.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have made a place where the whole world can live. We know this is really true when we see San Antonio, one of America’s foremost tourist destinations (all of which are havens of sense gratification), turning to Kṛṣṇa consciousness due to your sweet mercy. Here tourists flock to the famous River Walk for sense gratification, and suddenly they see Hare Kṛṣṇa devotees chanting. To our great surprise, many of these people actually join in the grand saṅkīrtana along the river. It’s amazing!

People also come here to see one of the largest annual parades in America—the Battle of Flowers Parade—in which over four hundred groups participate. The whole city takes half a day or even a full day off work to join this spectacular parade. Hundreds of thousands line the sidewalks, and the event is broadcast over all the major TV channels. During the parade many devotees chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, riding along on a float with Lord Jagannātha. Many thousands hear the holy names, and countless more hear and see us on TV.

This is all by your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for if you had not come to these shores on the Jaladuta to save us from the ocean of birth and death, we would have drowned in the endless ocean of māyā and karma. With your powerful example you gave us a glimpse of the wonderful spiritual world, and today our hearts overflow with joy just to relish a drop of the unlimited pastimes and qualities you displayed during your earthly years. Our mind gets overwhelmed by the thoughts of Kṛṣṇa’s true representative, who displays such matchless divine qualities.

The entire population of Kali-yuga should be very grateful to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We are, and in fact we feel eternally indebted to you for showing us the path to save our souls. We devotees here in San Antonio would also like to assist you in your monumental mission, and like squirrels in Lord Rāmacandra’s army, we shall continue to struggle to contribute whatever service we can render out of gratitude for all you have given us. We can try to please you on your appearance day by giving this precious gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to as many souls in San Antonio as possible.

Please give us your mercy and strength so that we can continue our service to you, life after life. Haribol!

Your unqualified servants in San Antonio, Texas, USA.

## ISKCON of Silicon Valley

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

There is growing concern in the world about economic inequality, the rapidly widening gap between the rich and the poor.

A January 2014 report by Oxfam claims that the combined assets of just 85 individuals is equal to the wealth of the bottom 50% of the world’s population, or about 3.5 billion people. What these statistics don’t show, however, is that 99.99% of the world’s population is spiritually poor.

Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī tells how real wealth is to be measured:

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu asked Rāmānanda Rāya, “Of the many capitalists who possess great riches, who is the topmost?”

Rāmānanda Rāya replied, “He who is richest in love for Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa is the greatest capitalist.” (*Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 8.247)

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you came to make people truly wealthy by distributing the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* which removes all obstacles on the spiritual path and awakens pure love for Kṛṣṇa in the hearts of those who regularly hear from it.

Because of your campaign, people will soon come to know that *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is more valuable than all the wealth of Kuvera, the heavenly treasurer, what to speak of the combined assets of the world’s richest 85 people. Factually, no one can fathom the value of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* because it is Kṛṣṇa Himself.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when you first entered the United States you wrote on your customs form that your cargo—two hundred sets of First Canto *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* volumes—were meant as “gifts.” For this reason the customs officials allowed you to bring in your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatams* duty-free. (Had the customs officials known the real value of your payload, they would have been shocked!)

Inspired by how you painstakingly imported these treasures to the shores of America, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the devotees at ISKCON of Silicon Valley (ISV) have been enthusiastically going door-to-door to distribute them.

In the course of our canvassing, some friendly people invite us into their homes and give us an opportunity to introduce them to the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. As we do so, we realize the great challenge you wrote about while aboard the *Jaladuta,* en route to America:

Most of the population here is covered by the material modes of ignorance and passion. Absorbed in material life, they think themselves very happy and satisfied, and therefore they have no taste for the transcendental message of Vāsudeva. I do not know how they will be able to understand it.

In Book III, Chapter 25, of Plato’s *Republic,* Socrates compares this material universe to a cave and the residents of the material world to cave-dwellers chained by their legs and necks so they can’t go out. The cave-dwellers are habituated to living in darkness and take the confines of the cave and the shadows flickering on the cave’s walls as ultimate reality. When someone comes to bring a cave-dweller outside into the world of light, the cave-dweller protests: “My cave is the only reality.” He may even become violent toward his liberator.

Socrates says that liberators should take only gradual steps in teaching cave-dwellers about the greater world outside their caves; for if one is too abrupt, the cave-dweller will be overwhelmed and reject one’s help altogether.

As we enter the various cavelike dwellings that are people’s homes, we face the daunting task of convincing our hosts that we have brought them something of great value. This is a challenging task because our hosts often listen to us just to be polite, while secretly hoping that we will soon leave so they can go back to watching television. Or, they offer myriad objections to accepting the books, most commonly saying they have no time to read.

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is Kṛṣṇa’s incarnation in Kali-yuga, but because people are in ignorance they feel we are trying to burden them with something that will only take up valuable space in their homes. However, we know that if they take *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* it will become their most valuable possession by far.

To interest them in reading *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* we tell them some of its stories, like how a *brāhmaṇa* boy cursed the saintly emperor of the world, Parīkṣit, to die within seven days; how emperor Parīkṣit accepted the curse and gave up his kingdom to sit down on the bank of the Ganges to prepare himself for death; and how by God’s arrangement the fully enlightened adolescent boy Śukadeva Gosvāmī arrived just in time to teach Parīkṣit how to prepare himself for going back to Godhead upon leaving his body. We also remind them that we are all in a situation similar to Emperor Parīkṣit’s, since every one of us will soon face death.

While presenting the *Bhāgavatam* we remember how you began writing your *Bhāgavatam* purports in Vṛndāvana; how you personally distributed your books in India; how you brought hundreds of sets of the First Canto with you to America aboard the *Jaladuta;* and how you even sold a set to the *Jaladuta’s* captain. We remember how, upon your arrival in America, you tirelessly translated and wrote purports to each successive Canto, pausing only long enough to write a summary of the all-important Tenth Canto*,* your famous *Kṛṣṇa* book.

Remembering all this, we coax, encourage, flatter, smile, frown, and try anything to convince our hosts to buy the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and keep it in their homes.

We also introduce your *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* and explain how Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is the golden *avatāra* of Kṛṣṇa who taught the blissful path of *saṅkīrtana,* which leads to perfection. We tell how *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* is the most esoteric and highly advanced spiritual book in the world and that it is very rare.

When people do buy a set of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* or *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* volumes, we perform an installation ceremony for the books, as follows: First we place the books on a prominent shelf in their home and decorate them with flowers. Next, we perform an *ārati* to the books while doing *kīrtana*. Finally, we pass around the first volume of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and ask everyone in the family to read one paragraph aloud. In this way, everyone present personally reads from the book. Throughout the ceremony, we see how the recipients have become attentive after paying their hard-earned money for the books and that by reading them aloud everyone present is promoted to the mode goodness and beyond, just by hearing the nectarean vibration of your books.

Aboard the *Jaladuta* you wrote:

The words of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* are Your incarnation, and if a sober person repeatedly receives it with submissive aural reception, then he will be able to understand Your message.

Such sober persons are rare. But because you have instructed us to see to it that “every respectable person \[receive\] a full set of *Bhāgavatam* and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* in his home,” we continue to search for these respectable people. And when one of them accepts the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* or *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* we know that we’ve given them the startup capital they’ll need to develop their own spiritual enterprise and someday even make the journey back to Godhead.

To help these fortunate recipients take advantage of their newly found wealth, we also leave them with a simple chart that shows how many pages they can read each day to complete each set of books in a fixed period of time. For example, if they daily read forty-one pages of your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* they can finish all twelve cantos in just one year. Or, if they choose to read just eight pages a day, they will finish the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in five years.

In America, people who win lots of money in a state lottery are invited to attend a meeting of other newly-made lottery millionaires. We similarly invite all the new *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* owners to a gathering in their neighborhood, considering that they have won the spiritual lottery by purchasing your *Bhāgavatam* set. Many people enthusiastically attend these meetings, their lives having been uplifted by Lord Kṛṣṇa moving into their homes in His literary incarnation.

You once said, “When one book is published, I think I have conquered an empire.”

When we sell a full set of your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* or *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* volumes, *Śrīla Prabhupāda*, we declare a similar victory.

Regarding the conquering of empires, in a dialog with some of your disciples you ask them to not make you like Alexander the Great, who conquered many kingdoms but who then moved on so quickly to conquer other kingdoms that he lost control of the ones he’d already conquered. You say that you are concerned that by opening too many temples too quickly without proper management, they will likely fall apart.

Continuing the dialog, you say, “I am stressing, therefore, book-selling, because it is permanent.”

Appreciating your point, your main interlocutor in the conversation, Tamāl Krishna Goswami, responds, “Once a devotee sells one of your books, the book will act. The book establishes itself as a Deity within a person’s home.”

“Yes,” you reply.

As our devotees install more and more of these special Deities in people’s homes, we feel your mercy empowering us in all of our important services to you at ISV.

To name only a few, dozens of our devotees have now passed the Bhakti Śāstrī course, and many more are taking it; classes for children are swelling; Deity worship and cooking standards are increasing; outreach programs at universities, corporations, and homes are expanding; our Internet broadcasts are becoming more refined; our new temple team is making our new temple increasingly more beautiful and efficient; and there is much more. But perhaps best of all, the devotees in our community are fond of one another and remain united in their goal to please you.

We are therefore the most grateful recipients of the spiritual wealth you have bequeathed to us on behalf of the greatest of all venture capitalists, Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

In *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* Kavirāja Gosvāmī writes:

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is a wealthy capitalist possessing the touchstone of love of God. Not considering whether one is a proper or improper recipient, He gives His treasure to anyone and everyone. Thus He is the most munificent. \[*Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 2.81\]

We pray to increase the spiritual wealth you’ve given us by engaging more people in reading and distributing your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and in broadcasting the holy names of Kṛṣṇa. Please bless us on your divine appearance day so that we may continue to assist you in bringing the greatest spiritual fortune and affluence to all the people of Silicon Valley and beyond.

Your servants at ISKCON of Silicon Valley, California, USA.  
(written by Vaiśeṣika Dāsa)

## Simhachalam

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you!

For us conditioned souls in this Kali-yuga, your lotus feet are the only shelter. Nowhere else can we learn how to cross the ocean of material existence. All the other options and solutions offered by bogus gurus, swamis, teachers, pseudoreligionists, and other misguiders simply bewilder seekers of the Absolute Truth. The true light in this dense, dark forest of wrong opinions shines in your magnificent books. Your books illuminate our vision, showing us the direct path to Goloka Vṛndāvana.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for saving us and engaging us in the mission of saving others. This is your gift—that we can offer your wonderful self in the form of your books to all the suffering people of this crazy age. Only by fulfilling the desire of the spiritual master can we obtain the mercy of Śrī Kṛṣṇa. For your true followers, what else is there to do but abide by your teachings?

Please empower us to bring as many people as possible to your lotus feet and, with ever-increasing enthusiasm and conviction, to distribute huge quantities of your wonderful books.

Your servants at Simhachalam, Jandelsbrunn, Gemany.  
(written by Ānanda Kṛṣṇa Dāsa)

## Taiwan

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

On this occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, we devotees in Taiwan would like to express our heartfelt gratitude for your painstaking efforts in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. Your faith in Lord Caitanya’s prophecy— that one day His *saṅkīrtana* movement would spread to each and every town and village in the world—brought devotees to this mostly Chinese-speaking country. You trained and empowered your disciples to travel all over the world and start preaching centers and temples to teach the principles of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and save the unfortunate souls struggling in this ever-deteriorating material world. As a result of such preaching efforts, every year we see more and more Taiwanese becoming Vaiṣṇavas and receiving formal initiation.

Taiwan is considered a so-called advanced country, with modern material technology, but deep within their hearts, the people here are unhappy. So many times in your books and lectures you point out that material advancement is no guarantee of happiness. The only ray of hope we see is in your books and your dedicated followers.

You always said, “Read my books and distribute my books.” We have several of your books that have been translated into Chinese, and we are distributing them all over the country. Whenever we see a new person coming to our programs and buying your books, we see your unlimited mercy manifested. Whenever we see a new person chanting the holy names of the Lord, we know for sure that this is only possible because of your causeless mercy.

For the past several years we have been taking part in the yearly International Book Fair in Taipei. Most of your books in English and Chinese are displayed very prominently.

Also, for a couple of years we have been holding annual retreats, where devotees get together for a few days and emphasize the chanting of the holy names in the association of devotees.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you made this process so easy for us. You said, “Just chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, dance, and take *kṛṣṇa*-*prasādam,* and your life will be successful.” But we are so fallen, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that we need constant reminders through your books and through your disciples who, following in your footsteps, travel all over the world to give association and guidance to fallen souls.

This year, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we plan to hold a Ratha-yātrā in Taipei in a big way. We sincerely pray to the Lord and seek your blessings so we may achieve the goal of attracting many more devotees to your mission here.

We, the members of ISKCON Taiwan, wish to again offer you our deepest gratitude for everything you have given us, and we pray for your continued blessings and mercy so we can more vigorously push on the *saṅkīrtana* movement in this country.

Your humble and insignificant servants at ISKCON Taiwan.

## Trivandrum

I Need Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Foot Dust!  
*One speck of Prabhupāda’s foot dust*—  
*It is all I want*,  
to place it on my tongue  
*so it will* always speak the glories  
of the Lord.  
*In this way the grain will multiply*,  
to storm with compassion  
*and love* all humankind—  
His Divine Grace so desired.  
*And* two more specks I need  
*from his lotus feet*,  
*to put* ’em in my ears  
*so they’ll* properly hear  
the chanting, the sound  
that he so mercifully  
has brought down.  
And one more speck  
*I wish to have*,  
To keep it in my heart  
So this rascal and crook  
*will become* a worthy recipient  
of the message in his books.  
Oh, one more grain  
*of dust* I beg to receive  
*from his* feet—  
to hold it in my hands  
*so* they’ll give, give, give  
as he tirelessly did.  
*Still* two more specks I desire—  
*to place* ’em on my eyes  
*so they’ll* see in every condition  
the way to serve with bhakti  
*his transcendental* mission.  
*And once more*, again  
*I plead* for one grain  
*of his* foot dust, and with faith  
I will treasure it in my mind  
so as to always be reminded  
*of the Lord and His Name*,  
*’*cause for spreading it  
His Divine Grace came.  
I remain a beggar  
*of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s* foot dust  
in those streets of love  
he has opened for all of us.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants in Trivandrum, Kerala, India.  
(written by Mādhava-latā Devī Dāsī)

## Tucson

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*I was only twenty-three* when I last saw your face.  
Some details are fading—can’t allow them to erase.  
I kept a picture of us, but can’t recall the place;  
These precious few encounters I desire to embrace.  
What was that verse? What was that quote?  
What was that example? Let me check my notes.  
Please keep my mind intact and my faculties afloat;  
Let your pastimes be fresh and your words not remote.  
Don’t let me forget your compassion and grace,  
Your mercy in saving the whole human race.  
The clock is advancing, no time left to waste;  
Allow me to hold firm to that sweet higher taste.  
That amazing story—it’s near the edge of my throat;  
*That melody, that rāga—*what was that note?  
My memory is slipping; I need to stay afloat;  
I desire to remember everything that you wrote.  
Let my devotion be indelible, just like India ink,  
Remembrances flowing so I don’t have to think.  
Through my passage to Kṛṣṇa, you are my link;  
The entrance to the spiritual sky is right on the brink.  
Fortunately your devotees penned your every word;  
We have your recordings, CDs, and videos transferred.  
So even though my memory may become foggy and blurred,  
From your lotus mouth into my heart the holy name was heard.  
The world was your stage,  
You came in old age,  
Didn’t ask for a wage.  
You were the sage  
Of the Lower East Side rage.  
*You taught bhakti* in the Iron Age;  
The youth you engaged,  
The materialists you enraged,  
You saved us as we came of age.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please keep us always under your lotus feet. Guide us, inspire us, and let us be a part of fulfilling your mission.

Your servants at ISKCON Tucson, Arizona.  
*(*written by Sandāminī Devī Dāsī)

## Ujjain

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Every year we devotees of ISKCON have this wonderful opportunity to offer our lives, our hearts, our services, and our very selves at your divine lotus feet through the medium of this Vyāsa-pūjā offering. *Śrīla Prabhupāda*, your mercy is all we are made of.

Every incident in your glorious life is most exemplary and instructive. You narrated an amazing incident that occurred in Calcutta on December 12, 1942. There was heavy bombing in the city, and almost all the people had vacated. You could hear the bombs drop, but rather than being afraid, you enjoyed the sounds like one would enjoy fireworks! You were chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, and you felt that if you died then, it would be wonderful to die chanting the holy names. The experience increased your resolve to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness, because you knew that it was a lack of Kṛṣṇa consciousness that was the root cause of all the chaos that prevailed. Soon thereafter, in 1944, you published the first issue of *Back to Godhead* magazine.

Several lessons can be learned from this incident. First, a devotee has to be tolerant of the material situation, which is full of dangers at every step. Second, only by taking shelter of Kṛṣṇa through His holy names can one attain true fearlessness. Third, a devotee learns lessons and derives inspiration to serve Kṛṣṇa even in the worst possible situation, which, rather than being a source of trouble, in fact enhances his resolve to serve the Supreme Lord. This is just one of many incidents in your life that illustrate how a true *ācārya* teaches by example.

It is interesting to note how you lived through those bombings simply to provide shelter to the whole world suffering from the “bombing raids” of Kali. The International Society for Krishna Consciousness is your wonderful arrangement—a house in which the whole world can live and be protected from the dangers of Kali-yuga and the powerful attacks of *Māyā*. Your Society offers us the opportunity to associate with so many wonderful devotees, to be sheltered by their association, to engage in pure devotional service in a wide variety of ways. To accommodate the proclivities of various individuals, ISKCON provides preaching opportunities in such novel ways as book distribution, college preaching, Nāma-haṭṭas, prison preaching, and even Internet preaching.

By your spiritual potency, preaching is flourishing in and around Ujjain. ISKCON Ujjain is conducting programs in practically all the prominent colleges of Madhya Pradesh, and there are several hundred youth seriously practicing Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Congregational preaching is expanding through the Bhakti Vriksha Program.

To further educate and train our devotees, we are conducting the Bhakti Shastri program for our temple devotees and congregation on a yearly basis. ISKCON Ujjain is one of the most prominent attractions of the city, and people flock into the temple by the thousands every day. Through regular book distribution, *harināma,* and our traveling *saṅkīrtana* party, our humble efforts to serve you are meeting with success, all by your divine grace. Ultimately, by the divine association and guidance given by your spiritual son His Holiness Bhakti Charu Swami, the devotees of Ujjain feel spiritually sheltered and inspired to serve the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa.

We recently organized a Kīrtana Mela for three days, where throughout the day and into the night the devotees simply took shelter of the *kīrtana* of the holy names, totally absorbing themselves in the divine nectar. Reputed *kīrtana* leaders such as His Holiness Bhakti Bhṛṅga Govinda Swami and His Grace Mādhava Prabhu led the *kīrtanas,* along with our local GBC, His Holiness Bhakti Charu Swami, and other devotees.

Last December Bhakti Charu Swami organized a spiritual retreat attended by devotees from across the globe. The topic was the *Uddhava-gītā,* from the Eleventh Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. Both the *kīrtana*-*mela* and the retreat were truly international events, and many devotees are requesting that they be repeated annually. It was truly a great fortune for us to have the opportunity to host these events and thus serve senior devotees, as well as to absorb ourselves in the transcendental sound vibration of *hari-kathā* and *harināma*-*kīrtana*.

Under the guidance of Bhakti Charu Swami, preaching in local villages has begun, with a very positive response. The main aim of these activities is to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the numerous villages of Madhya Pradesh.

We owe it all to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and pray for more and more such opportunities to serve you and your dear servants.

Thank you very much for everything you have given us.

Your humble servants at ISKCON Ujjain, India.

## Vedic Farm (Mauritius)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble *daṇḍavats* at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you!

I am a fallen disciple of your beloved disciple His Holiness Girirāja Swami. I have been asked to write an offering to glorify Your Divine Grace, but you are so exalted that I do not know how to begin. Therefore I am humbly begging you to be kind to me and bestow your mercy upon me so I may understand how to glorify you. I would be very grateful to you if you did, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I am not good at anything whatsoever, but I love you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, from the core of my heart for what you have done for us. Your gifts are beyond comparison.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, from your birth till your departure your activities were all transcendental. Why? Because you are a genuine representative of the Lord. Therefore your birth and activities were all transcendental, like Lord Kṛṣṇa’s. In the *Bhagavad-gītā* (4.9) the Lord says:

*janma karma ca me divyam evaṁ yo vetti tattvataḥ*  
*tyaktvā dehaṁ punar janma naiti mām eti so 'rjuna*

**“One who knows the transcendental nature of My appearance and activities does not, upon leaving the body, take his birth again in this material world, but attains My eternal abode, O Arjuna.”**

You once said, “My only credit is that I have not changed anything. I have presented \[the philosophy\] as it is.” You have given the whole world Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, which no one else ever did in the history of the world. You did this by giving us His holy names—Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. These are your matchless gifts.

In your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.16.32–33 you write:

In this age, when everything is polluted by the contamination of Kali, it is instructed in the scriptures and preached by Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu that by chanting the holy name of the Lord we can at once be free from contamination and gradually rise to the status of transcendence and go back to Godhead. The offenseless chanter of the holy name of the Lord is as auspicious as the Lord Himself, and the movement of pure devotees of the Lord all over the world can at once change the troublesome face of the world. Only by the propagation of the chanting of the holy name of the Lord can we be immune from all effects of the Age of Kali.

In your early twenties you became a follower of Gandhi. During that period you came in contact with your great spiritual master, Śrīla *Bhaktisiddhānta* Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, who asked you to preach the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu all over the world. You challenged him: “Who will hear your Caitanya’s message? We are a dependent country. First India must become independent. How can we spread Indian culture if we are under British rule?”

Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta replied boldly that Kṛṣṇa consciousness could not wait for a change in Indian politics—the urgency of preaching *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* did not depend on who ruled India. Kṛṣṇa consciousness was so important—so exclusively important—that it could not wait. The rule of one power or another was a temporary situation, but the eternal reality is Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and the real self is the spirit soul. No manmade political system, therefore, could actually help humanity. This is the verdict of the Vedic scriptures and the disciplic line of spiritual masters. Although everyone is an eternal servant of God, when one takes himself to be the temporary body and regards the nation of this birth as worshipable, he comes under illusion. The leaders and followers of the world’s political movements, including India’s movement for independence (*swarāj*), were simply cultivating this illusion. But real welfare work, whether individual, social, or political, should prepare a person for his next life by helping him reestablish his eternal relationship with the Supreme Lord.

Soon after your meeting with Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, you left Gandhi and became a staunch follow of Śrīla *Bhaktisiddhānta* Sarasvatī. This meeting took place in the year 1922. After preparing yourself properly and publishing the three volumes of the First Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, in 1965 you prepared to depart for America to execute the mission of your spiritual master. You had no money at all, so you begged a free passage on a freighter owned by Mrs. Sumati Morarjee. Impressing her with your strong determination to fulfill your spiritual master’s order, you were able to convince her to offer the ticket.

During your trip to America you had two heart attacks, but Lord Kṛṣṇa consoled and pacified you in a dream. Your only solace was *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* in which you tasted the nectar of Lord Caitanya’s *līlā*. Thus you left India to execute the order of Śrīla *Bhaktisiddhānta* Sarasvatī, in pursuance of Lord Caitanya’s order. You wrote that you had no qualification but that you had taken the risk just to carry out the order of His Divine Grace. You depended fully on the mercy of the Lord and your spiritual master, so far away from Vṛndāvana.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, for months after landing in America in September 1965 you struggled alone, but you never gave up. You were very compassionate to everyone you met. You helped them in many ways. Dr. Ramamurti Mishra said the following:

\[Swamiji\] really knocked me down with love. He was really an incarnation of love. My body had become a skeleton, and he really brought me back to life—his cooking, and especially his love and his devotion to Lord Kṛṣṇa. I was very lazy in the matter of cooking, but he would get up and have ready.

You would write many letters to contacts in India and ask for help, but no one helped. You struggled alone, but you never gave up. You had unbreakable determination.

You wrote to Sumati Morarjee in April of 1966:

I have come here in this old age neither for sight seeing nor for any personal interest. It is for the interest of the entire humanity that I am trying to implement the science of Krishna which will actually make them happy. So it is the duty of every devotee of Lord Krishna to help me by all means.

You wrote to *Śrī*pāda Tīrtha Mahārāja for help, but there was no reply. You wrote to others—and the reply was loud and clear: “We cannot help you.”

But because you were so determined and qualified, very soon Lord Kṛṣṇa rewarded you with the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. You made the hippies into happies, and from 1966 to 1977 you preached worldwide, founding 108 temples and attracting thousands of followers. You translated more than nine cantos of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, the entire *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, and the *Bhagavad-gītā,* explaining each verse with your erudite purports. You also gave us *Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme personality of Godhead,* a summary study of the Tenth Canto, and numerous other books—small, medium, and large.

As for Kṛṣṇa’s devotees, you glorified them as very intelligent because they have chosen to serve Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. By watering the root of a tree, you taught, one nourishes all the tree’s twigs, branches, leaves, and fruits. In the same way, you taught (quoting the *Bhāgavatam*) that by worshiping Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the root of the tree of all creation, one worships all the demigods and -goddesses.

As for the chanting of the holy name, you said that the holy name is identical with the Lord. Or in other words, the Lord is present in His holy name. When the devotees have some free time, you said, they have a very important thing to do: chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. Anyone can chant—old, young, sick, etc.—without restriction.

As for books, you said they are the basis. This is the greatest contribution you made. Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for this gift. Your books have saved the lives of many people around the world.

As for temples, they are the places where the Lord resides and where the devotees worship Him and offer Him *bhoga,* the remnants of which they enjoy as *prasādam*.

As for *prasādam,* who does not like to eat delicious food? Distributing *prasādam* is the best way to attract people to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Many devotees have come because of *prasādam.* When we serve guests *prasādam,* they become very favorable.

As for preaching, you said it is the essence. You established outdoor and indoor preaching. In this way, everyone is a preacher in ISKCON. You established ISKCON to make first-class *brāhmaṇa* preachers. Because of the preaching and the performance of *harināma,* the movement is expanding very strongly. One who preaches has life. One who does not preach is dead.

This is my offering to you, *Śrīla Prabhupāda*, a meager attempt to enumerate your infinite glories. I humbly beg you to excuse any offenses I may have committed here, and I beg you to please accept my humble offering at your lotus feet. I also beg you to bless me and keep me in the good association of devotees. Thank you very much.

Śrīla Prabhupāda kī jaya! All glories to you!

Your devotees at the ISKCON Vedic Farm and Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira in Mauritius.  
(written by Rāma-vijaya Dāsa)

## Villaggio Hare Krishna

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you, my divine eternal master, who cured my eyes of blindness by giving me real spiritual vision.

Recently I had the good fortune to read a letter written on a palm leaf by Narottama Dāsa Ṭhākura to Śrīla *Jīva* *Gosvāmī*. The letter contained the annual book distribution scores of Śrīnivāsa Ācārya, Narottama Dāsa, and Śyāmānanda Prabhu for the year ending on Gaura Pūrṇimā of 1501 Śakābda (A.D. 1579). Narottama Dāsa concludes his letter with the following emotional comment:

Until we are able to develop another type of technology to print the books, the difficulty involved in creating books by hand (which is being neglected) will force us to postpone most of our book distribution program. We are sure that one day some magnanimous person will come and develop the movement of ecstatic love of Lord *Caitanya* Mahāprabhu, distributing books all over the world. Until then, what can we poor, bereft, and abominable persons do?

The magnanimous person mentioned in this letter is Your Divine Grace, and Narottama Dāsa Ṭhākura could foresee the course of future events: from the very moment you stepped off the *Jaladuta* onto American soil, the world would never be the same.

As Christopher Columbus’s discovery of America in 1492 opened new frontiers to the world, Your Divine Grace’s arrival in America in 1965 opened new scenarios and life prospects no one could have imagined.

Your transcendental touch changed the course of history, and you predicted that this change would continue for the next ten thousands years due to the blossoming of the *saṅkīrtana* movement, and that your books would continue to be the foundation for the future spiritual progress of humanity.

This prediction is in your books, like the prophecy expressed by Narottama Dāsa Ṭhākura in his letter, and in due course of time your prediction will be fulfilled in its entirety. Actually, we can say that it has already been fulfilled by the supreme authority. As Kṛṣṇa told Arjuna, “The battle is already won by My decision, but you take the credit.”

With your presence you ignited a revolution in people’s consciousness that, like fire, is spreading wildly all over the planet and touching thousands and millions of hearts, which are unlocking to welcome your mercy. The lives of these fortunate souls will never be the same.

I feel honored to be part of the dream of Narottama Dāsa Ṭhākura and of all the other glorious *ācāryas* of the *Gauḍīya*-Vaiṣṇava *sampradāya.* Their dream is coming true thanks to the fortitude and efforts of you, *Śrīla Prabhupāda*, our great *senāpati bhakta,* our eminent general, who took up the leadership of the “movement of ecstatic love of Lord *Caitanya* Mahāprabhu, distributing books all over the world.”

Thanks to the enthusiasm and dedication of you, our founder-*ācārya*, that dream is now an established reality: people all over the world are chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*.

I only pray to be blessed with a drop of the urgency for spreading this mission felt by our great *ācāryas*. Sometimes, since old age is quickly advancing, thoughts enter my mind of retiring to Vṛndāvana or another holy spot to chant and read peacefully. Then the vivid image of Your Divine Grace renouncing the serene life of Vṛndāvana at the age of seventy to come to the insidious Western countries at the risk of your life strongly fills my mind, and I lower my head in shame.

Actually, I am very happy to serve in one of your innumerable temples, transcendental embassies of the divine motherland known as Śrīdhām Māyāpur.
